The paper contributes the MultiLabeled Cancer Adverse Drug Reaction and Summarization (MCADRS) dataset, which includes pharmacovigilance posts, extracted labels for drug names, ADEs, severity, adversity, and summaries for each drug.  GASCADE combines the information extraction capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) with the summarization power of the T5 model. The framework uses alignment techniques like Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) on a synthetic dataset. Experiments demonstrate GASCADE's superior performance validated through both automated and human evaluations. The paper emphasizes enhancing drug-related decision-making and personalized cancer care.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel *task* of grouped ADE summarization specifically for cancer pharmacovigilance. Existing research has focused primarily on general diseases, not the nuances of cancer treatment. The *dataset* MCADRS is a significant contribution, filling a gap in cancer-specific pharmacovigilance resources. The use of DPO for alignment in this *specific domain with synthetic data generation* is also a worthwhile experiment.

*   **Significance:**  Effective summarization of ADEs has clear real-world implications for healthcare providers, enabling better anticipation of side effects and informed medical decisions. The emphasis on severity allows for prioritization of critical information. The potential for personalized cancer care is a compelling longer-term impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined Task:** Clearly presents the problem and task being addressed.
    *   **Dataset Creation:** Provides a new, valuable resource for the community.  The annotation process (involving medical professionals) strengthens credibility.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Uses both automated metrics and human evaluation. Includes ablation studies to understand the contribution of each component.
    *   **Alignment Technique:**  Addresses the hallucination/reliability of LLMs by using DPO with a synthetic dataset for alignment.
    *   **Focus on a Specific Domain (Cancer):** Adds value and novelty compared to general approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic DPO Dataset:** The creation of the non-preferred summaries using GPT-4 is a potential limitation. The quality of these synthetic examples directly impacts the effectiveness of DPO. A more thorough analysis of the sensitivity to the synthetic data creation process would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited Baselines:** While the baselines are reasonably chosen, more specialized summarization techniques from the broader biomedical literature could have been included.
    *   **Dataset size**: Although the creation of a cancer-specific dataset is an important contribution, the size could be a limitation for training deep learning models.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper demonstrates solid technical execution, and addresses an important problem with real-world impact. The novelty primarily lies in the cancer-specific application and the specific combination of techniques (grouping, summarization, and DPO alignment in this context). While not groundbreaking from a purely algorithmic standpoint, the application and resource (dataset) are substantial. The use of synthetic data for DPO alignment also adds an interesting layer but could benefit from a deeper discussion/analysis. The main reservation lies in the potential limitations introduced by the method used to generate the DPO data and the relatively small size of the dataset.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MoDE: Mixture of Diffusion Experts for Any Occluded Face Recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04306v1)
- **Authors**: Qiannan Fan, Zhuoyang Li, Jitong Li, Chenyang Cao
- **Abstract**: With the continuous impact of epidemics, people have become accustomed to wearing masks. However, most current occluded face recognition (OFR) algorithms lack prior knowledge of occlusions, resulting in poor performance when dealing with occluded faces of varying types and severity in reality. Recognizing occluded faces is still a significant challenge, which greatly affects the convenience of people's daily lives. In this paper, we propose an identity-gated mixture of diffusion experts (MoDE) for OFR. Each diffusion-based generative expert estimates one possible complete image for occluded faces. Considering the random sampling process of the diffusion model, which introduces inevitable differences and variations between the inpainted faces and the real ones. To ensemble effective information from multi-reconstructed faces, we introduce an identity-gating network to evaluate the contribution of each reconstructed face to the identity and adaptively integrate the predictions in the decision space. Moreover, our MoDE is a plug-and-play module for most existing face recognition models. Extensive experiments on three public face datasets and two datasets in the wild validate our advanced performance for various occlusions in comparison with the competing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MoDE (Mixture of Diffusion Experts), a novel framework for occluded face recognition (OFR). MoDE leverages multiple diffusion-based generative experts to reconstruct occluded faces. An identity-gating network (ID-Gate) adaptively integrates the information from these reconstructed faces in the decision space. This module can be plugged into existing face recognition models. Experiments demonstrate the method's superior performance on both artificially and real-world occluded face datasets compared to state-of-the-art techniques.  The paper argues that MoDE can handle diverse occlusion types and offers a robust approach to OFR.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the *combination* of techniques rather than any single completely new component. Using diffusion models for image inpainting is not entirely new, but the specific application to occluded face recognition and the *mixture of experts with an identity-gated fusion mechanism* is a relatively novel contribution. Most prior work in OFR has focused on either occlusion-robust feature extraction or using GANs for reconstruction. The paper demonstrates the diffusion models are effective for such task. Introducing ID-gate to utilize the different features obtained from various repainted image is also a novel attempt. The implementation and design are not complex, yet it is effective for addressing the problem of occluded face recognition.

*   **Significance:** The significance is moderate. Occluded face recognition is a practical and important problem, particularly with the increased use of masks.  Improving performance in this area directly impacts real-world applications like security, authentication, and access control. The paper's improvement over existing methods, especially on real-world occluded datasets, demonstrates its potential value.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical results: The paper presents convincing evidence of MoDE's superiority across multiple datasets and occlusion types.
    *   Plug-and-play design: The modularity of MoDE makes it easy to integrate with existing face recognition systems.
    *   Addresses a practical problem: The focus on real-world occlusions enhances the method's practical relevance.
    *   Thorough ablation study: Ablation experiments carefully study the effectiveness of key components.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Incremental advancement: While the overall approach is novel, most individual components exist. The novelty comes from the specific combination.
    *   Computational cost: Diffusion models are computationally intensive, which could be a limitation for real-time applications. The paper doesn't adequately address this.
    *   Lack of theoretical justification: The paper lacks rigorous mathematical analysis to justify the effectiveness of ID-Gate.
    *   The ID gate design is quite simple, and the performance in Table III are not obviously better.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by demonstrating the effectiveness of diffusion models and the mixture of experts approach for occluded face recognition. Other researchers might build upon this framework to develop more efficient or robust OFR systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of occluded face recognition. The use of diffusion models combined with a mixture of experts and an adaptive gating mechanism constitutes a novel approach with strong empirical results. However, the individual components are not entirely new, and the paper could benefit from a more thorough analysis of the method's computational complexity and the theoretical justification of ID-Gate. Therefore, the score reflects a meaningful but not groundbreaking advancement.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multi-turn Consistent Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04320v1)
- **Authors**: Zijun Zhou, Yingying Deng, Xiangyu He, Weiming Dong, Fan Tang
- **Abstract**: Many real-world applications, such as interactive photo retouching, artistic content creation, and product design, require flexible and iterative image editing. However, existing image editing methods primarily focus on achieving the desired modifications in a single step, which often struggles with ambiguous user intent, complex transformations, or the need for progressive refinements. As a result, these methods frequently produce inconsistent outcomes or fail to meet user expectations. To address these challenges, we propose a multi-turn image editing framework that enables users to iteratively refine their edits, progressively achieving more satisfactory results. Our approach leverages flow matching for accurate image inversion and a dual-objective Linear Quadratic Regulators (LQR) for stable sampling, effectively mitigating error accumulation. Additionally, by analyzing the layer-wise roles of transformers, we introduce a adaptive attention highlighting method that enhances editability while preserving multi-turn coherence. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our framework significantly improves edit success rates and visual fidelity compared to existing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of consistent image editing in multi-turn scenarios, where users iteratively refine edits through multiple prompts. It proposes a novel framework based on rectified flow models, enhanced with a dual-objective Linear Quadratic Regulator (LQR) to maintain long-term coherence and control image distribution.  The framework further incorporates an adaptive attention guidance mechanism, analyzing transformer layers to focus editing on salient regions and balancing stability with editability.  The authors demonstrate improvements in edit success rates and visual fidelity compared to existing single-step and multi-turn editing methods through quantitative and qualitative experiments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines several existing techniques (rectified flow, LQR control, attention analysis) in a novel way to address a specific problem in image editing. While the individual components are not groundbreaking, the integration to achieve multi-turn consistency is a key contribution. The dual-objective LQR and adaptive attention mechanism are also new, and effectively address error accumulation in multi-turn editing. The idea of analyzing the internal layers of transformers (DiT) to guide attention is an interesting adaptation.

*   **Significance:** Multi-turn image editing is a practical and important area for real-world applications like product design and content creation. The proposed framework offers a significant advancement over existing methods by enabling iterative refinement of images while preserving original features and consistency. By improving editing success rates and visual fidelity, this work contributes to more controllable and user-friendly image editing workflows.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed framework combining multiple techniques.
    *   Quantitative and qualitative evaluations showing improvement over baselines.
    *   Ablation studies demonstrating the effectiveness of the key components.
    *   The integration of GPT-4 turbo for generating extended dataset for multi-turn evaluation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation is somewhat limited by the dataset generation process.  Relying on GPT-4 turbo introduces a potential bias, and the authors acknowledge ChatGPT-related errors impacted the dataset's size.
    *   The reliance on multiple existing techniques makes the framework complex, which reduces its accessibility.
    *   The limitations of editing iteration, as claimed by the authors is a significant issue. A greater number of evaluation is required.
    *   The writing and presentation are strong, but require more explanation on experimental findings.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to impact the development of more interactive and controllable image editing tools. The methods for maintaining coherence across multiple edits and adapting attention could be valuable for future research in this area.

*   **Areas for Improvement:**
    *   Explore alternative methods for dataset generation to reduce bias and expand the number of editing rounds.
    *   Investigate ways to simplify the framework and make it more computationally efficient.
    *   Conduct more comprehensive comparisons with other multi-turn editing approaches.
    *   The lack of focus on fairness and ethical concerns is a minor weakness, although not directly related to the technical contribution.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid technical contribution to multi-turn image editing. The combination of techniques to address consistency and editability is well-motivated and demonstrably effective. The dual-objective LQR and adaptive attention are novel and contribute meaningfully to the framework's performance.  While the individual components have roots in prior work, the synthesis and application to the multi-turn editing problem are significant. The main limitations lie in the dataset creation process, evaluation scope, and complexity of the framework. These limitations prevent a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CountDiffusion: Text-to-Image Synthesis with Training-Free Counting-Guidance Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04347v1)
- **Authors**: Yanyu Li, Pencheng Wan, Liang Han, Yaowei Wang, Liqiang Nie, Min Zhang
- **Abstract**: Stable Diffusion has advanced text-to-image synthesis, but training models to generate images with accurate object quantity is still difficult due to the high computational cost and the challenge of teaching models the abstract concept of quantity. In this paper, we propose CountDiffusion, a training-free framework aiming at generating images with correct object quantity from textual descriptions. CountDiffusion consists of two stages. In the first stage, an intermediate denoising result is generated by the diffusion model to predict the final synthesized image with one-step denoising, and a counting model is used to count the number of objects in this image. In the second stage, a correction module is used to correct the object quantity by changing the attention map of the object with universal guidance. The proposed CountDiffusion can be plugged into any diffusion-based text-to-image (T2I) generation models without further training. Experiment results demonstrate the superiority of our proposed CountDiffusion, which improves the accurate object quantity generation ability of T2I models by a large margin.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "CountDiffusion: Text-to-Image Synthesis with Training-Free Counting-Guidance Diffusion" proposes a novel, training-free method to improve the accuracy of object quantity in text-to-image (T2I) synthesis.  The approach, named CountDiffusion, uses a two-stage process. First, it generates an intermediate image using a standard diffusion model.  A counting model (specifically, Grounded SAM) analyzes this intermediate image to determine the number of objects present.  Second, a correction module adjusts the attention maps within the diffusion model to add or remove objects, guiding the final image generation towards the target quantity. The method is designed to be plug-and-play with existing diffusion-based T2I models. The authors also create a dataset using LLMs to facilitate evaluation. The paper presents quantitative and qualitative results demonstrating improvements in object count accuracy over existing T2I models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of a training-free counting guidance mechanism is moderately novel. While leveraging external tools (like Grounded SAM) isn't groundbreaking, the way the attention maps are manipulated within a diffusion model *without* retraining is a distinct contribution.  The two-stage approach and the implementation of universal guidance to address multi-loss problems offer a worthwhile refinement of existing diffusion-based approaches. However, the extent of novelty needs to be viewed with caution because the individual building blocks of the method are well-established. The combination of these elements and adapting them for counting-guided generation constitutes the novelty.
* **Significance:** The significance of addressing object quantity accuracy in T2I is substantial.  Many current T2I models struggle with precise control over object counts, a common issue that reduces the usability and reliability of these systems. Improving accuracy in this area makes T2I models more predictable and controllable, expanding their potential applications. The training-free nature is a significant benefit because it allows the method to be readily applied to a wide range of pre-trained models.  The multi-loss universal guidance could be extended to other control modalities. The limitations on handling a large number of objects are a notable drawback.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Training-Free:** Avoids the computational burden of training a new model.
    * **Plug-and-Play:**  Can be integrated with existing diffusion models easily.
    * **Clear Evaluation:** The quantitative and qualitative results show a definite improvement in counting accuracy.  The creation of a new dataset shows a commitment to rigorous evaluation.
    * **Multi-Loss Approach:** The universal guidance approach addresses a key limitation of using multiple losses.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Grounded SAM:**  Performance is directly tied to the accuracy of the external counting model (Grounded SAM).  Errors in Grounded SAM will propagate.
    * **Limited Object Counts:**  The paper acknowledges a limitation in handling a large number of objects.  This restricts the applicability of the method to simpler scenes.
    * **Intermediate Image Quality:**  The method relies on an intermediate image, and the one-step denoising results in lower image quality. This could potentially affect the final output image quality.  Although the method addresses it through the steps that follow, the approach itself is fundamentally tied to the quality of the initial estimate.
    * **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** A more rigorous theoretical analysis of how the attention map manipulation affects the diffusion process would be beneficial. The paper relies on empirical evidence.

**Justification for Score:**

The CountDiffusion paper presents a worthwhile contribution to the field of T2I synthesis by addressing the practically important issue of object quantity control.  The training-free nature and plug-and-play design make it a valuable tool for improving existing T2I models. The proposed universal guidance approach also improves the applicability of the universal guidance method. The limitations, particularly the reliance on the external counting model and the struggle with high object counts, temper the overall impact. Considering the combination of novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, the method represents a solid improvement over existing approaches, but with clearly defined limitations.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Benchmarking LLMs' Swarm intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04364v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Ruan, Mowen Huang, Ji-Rong Wen, Hao Sun
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) show potential for complex reasoning, yet their capacity for emergent coordination in Multi-Agent Systems (MAS) when operating under strict constraints-such as limited local perception and communication, characteristic of natural swarms-remains largely unexplored, particularly concerning the nuances of swarm intelligence. Existing benchmarks often do not fully capture the unique challenges of decentralized coordination that arise when agents operate with incomplete spatio-temporal information. To bridge this gap, we introduce SwarmBench, a novel benchmark designed to systematically evaluate the swarm intelligence capabilities of LLMs acting as decentralized agents. SwarmBench features five foundational MAS coordination tasks within a configurable 2D grid environment, forcing agents to rely primarily on local sensory input (k x k view) and local communication. We propose metrics for coordination effectiveness and analyze emergent group dynamics. Evaluating several leading LLMs in a zero-shot setting, we find significant performance variations across tasks, highlighting the difficulties posed by local information constraints. While some coordination emerges, results indicate limitations in robust planning and strategy formation under uncertainty in these decentralized scenarios. Assessing LLMs under swarm-like conditions is crucial for realizing their potential in future decentralized systems. We release SwarmBench as an open, extensible toolkit-built upon a customizable and scalable physical system with defined mechanical properties. It provides environments, prompts, evaluation scripts, and the comprehensive experimental datasets generated, aiming to foster reproducible research into LLM-based MAS coordination and the theoretical underpinnings of Embodied MAS. Our code repository is available at https://github.com/x66ccff/swarmbench.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Benchmarking LLMs' Swarm intelligence":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SwarmBench, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the swarm intelligence capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) acting as decentralized agents. SwarmBench comprises five foundational multi-agent coordination tasks (Pursuit, Synchronization, Foraging, Flocking, Transport) within a configurable 2D grid environment.  Critically, agents are constrained by limited local perception (a k x k view) and optional local communication. The paper proposes metrics for assessing coordination effectiveness and analyzes emergent group dynamics.  The authors evaluate several leading LLMs in a zero-shot setting, revealing significant performance variations across tasks and highlighting the challenges posed by local information constraints. They release SwarmBench as an open, extensible toolkit to foster reproducible research into LLM-based MAS coordination.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a significant gap in the evaluation of LLMs by focusing on their ability to coordinate in decentralized, swarm-like settings. While multi-agent benchmarks exist, they often lack the strict constraints (limited perception, communication) characteristic of natural swarms. SwarmBench is novel in its explicit design to evaluate emergent coordination under such conditions, forcing reliance on local cues and implicit strategies. The choice of tasks, inspired by natural swarm behaviors, adds to the novelty. This specific focus distinguishes it from more general multi-agent LLM benchmarks.

*   **Significance:** Assessing LLMs under swarm-like conditions is crucial for realizing their potential in future decentralized systems. The benchmark provides a dedicated platform to measure progress and guide future research towards developing LLMs capable of genuine collective intelligence in decentralized settings. The open-source toolkit, including environments, prompts, evaluation scripts, and comprehensive datasets, significantly contributes to fostering reproducible research. It enables systematic experimentation and facilitates the study of factors influencing LLM swarm behavior, a topic currently underexplored. The analysis of group dynamics, linking LLM actions to established collective intelligence concepts, offers valuable insights. The identification of key challenges, such as long-range planning and robust spatial reasoning under uncertainty, provides direction for future research efforts.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing benchmarks in evaluating LLM performance in decentralized swarm scenarios.
    *   **Well-Designed Benchmark:** SwarmBench is thoughtfully designed with tasks inspired by natural swarm intelligence, featuring a configurable 2D grid environment and a physics engine. The limited perception and communication constraints are crucial for studying emergent coordination.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a systematic evaluation of multiple LLMs across different tasks and analyzes group dynamics, offering valuable insights into LLM swarm behavior.
    *   **Open-Source Toolkit:** The release of SwarmBench as an open-source toolkit promotes reproducibility and enables further research in this area.
    *   **Insightful Discussion:** The paper provides a detailed discussion of observed limitations and identifies key research directions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Simplified Environment:** The 2D grid environment, while facilitating focused investigation, might limit the transferability of findings to more complex, continuous 3D spaces. The physical interactions are greatly simplified compared to real-world environments.
    *   **Zero-Shot Evaluation:** The reliance on zero-shot evaluation, while providing a baseline, does not explore the potential of LLMs to learn and adapt in these environments.
    *   **Limited Communication Analysis:** The communication analysis is relatively basic and could be further enhanced by incorporating more sophisticated NLP techniques.
    *   **Specificity of tasks**: Although the tasks are well-motivated and grounded, the choice of task is relatively narrow and might not fully capture the capabilities of LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:** SwarmBench has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating LLM-based swarm intelligence. The findings will guide future research on developing LLMs capable of effective coordination in decentralized systems. The open-source toolkit will facilitate collaboration and accelerate progress in this area.

*   **Score Justification:**
The paper represents a solid contribution to the field by identifying a critical gap in LLM evaluation and introducing a novel benchmark specifically designed to address this gap. The limitations, while present, do not diminish the significance of the work as a starting point for further research. The comprehensive evaluation and open-source toolkit make this paper valuable for the community.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 7/10

### CDE-Mapper: Using Retrieval-Augmented Language Models for Linking Clinical Data Elements to Controlled Vocabularies
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04365v1)
- **Authors**: Komal Gilani, Marlo Verket, Christof Peters, Michel Dumontier, Hans-Peter Brunner-La Rocca, Visara Urovi
- **Abstract**: The standardization of clinical data elements (CDEs) aims to ensure consistent and comprehensive patient information across various healthcare systems. Existing methods often falter when standardizing CDEs of varying representation and complex structure, impeding data integration and interoperability in clinical research. We introduce CDE-Mapper, an innovative framework that leverages Retrieval-Augmented Generation approach combined with Large Language Models to automate the linking of CDEs to controlled vocabularies. Our modular approach features query decomposition to manage varying levels of CDEs complexity, integrates expert-defined rules within prompt engineering, and employs in-context learning alongside multiple retriever components to resolve terminological ambiguities. In addition, we propose a knowledge reservoir validated by a human-in-loop approach, achieving accurate concept linking for future applications while minimizing computational costs. For four diverse datasets, CDE-Mapper achieved an average of 7.2\% higher accuracy improvement compared to baseline methods. This work highlights the potential of advanced language models in improving data harmonization and significantly advancing capabilities in clinical decision support systems and research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CDE-Mapper, a novel Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG)-based framework designed to automate the process of linking clinical data elements (CDEs) to controlled vocabularies. CDE-Mapper addresses the challenges of inconsistent terminology and the complexity of composite CDEs, which existing methods often struggle to standardize. The framework employs a modular architecture including query decomposition, ensemble retrieval (leveraging sparse and dense embeddings), knowledge filtering, and a knowledge reservoir validated through a human-in-the-loop process.  The paper evaluates CDE-Mapper on four diverse datasets, showing improvements in accuracy compared to baseline methods (SapBERT, KRISS BERT, BioBERT-snomed, and PromptLink). The results highlight the potential of advanced language models for improving data harmonization in clinical decision support systems and research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper is moderate. While RAG is an established technique, the *specific application* of a modular RAG architecture to CDE linking, especially considering the complexity of *both* atomic and composite CDEs, is a contribution. The modularity, the integration of expert-defined rules, and the curated, validated knowledge reservoir enhance the RAG approach significantly.  The work extends beyond simple entity linking, tackling the nuances of clinical data standardization that are often glossed over. The emphasis on handling *composite* CDEs is a distinct advance.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the potential impact on clinical data interoperability and research.  Standardizing CDEs is crucial for accurate data integration, which, in turn, facilitates more reliable clinical decision support systems and reproducible research. CDE-Mapper appears to offer a promising pathway to improve data harmonization and therefore has some potential for real-world impact. The use of human-in-the-loop to validate the generated mapping is also a strength since clinical information can be misinterpreted.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive approach:** The modular RAG architecture and the inclusion of multiple retrieval mechanisms, knowledge filtering, and human validation are well-considered.
    *   **Handling composite CDEs:** The focus on standardizing both atomic and composite CDEs is a crucial advance that makes the work more relevant to real-world clinical data. The JSON output is another notable strength.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** The reported accuracy improvements across various datasets provide solid evidence for the effectiveness of the CDE-Mapper framework, even though some dataset differences should be explored.
    *   **Detailed ablation studies:** The ablation studies and component analysis demonstrate the importance of individual components and help to understand the architecture's effectiveness.
    *   **Case Study:** Helps to explain the effectiveness of the model in different challenging situations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:**  The paper mentions the computational cost limitations. The use of large language models implies significant computational resources, which may hinder practical adoption.  Further discussion on the scalability of the proposed approach in resource-constrained environments would be beneficial. While a Llama3.1 models are used, the fine-tuning for the medical domain will still be costly.
    *   **Knowledge Base Dependency:** The framework is heavily reliant on the quality and completeness of the controlled vocabularies used in the knowledge base.  The success of the linking depends on having relevant concepts represented. Datasets showing limited improvement imply that more information is needed in the knowledge reservoir. A more in-depth discussion of limitations and how to overcome them in the discussion section would be helpful.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to More Advanced Methods:** The baseline methods, while established, aren't necessarily the *most* cutting-edge. Comparing to a few more recent methods that address similar challenges in data integration or information extraction would strengthen the evaluation. PromptLink is one but other LLM based techniques such as SPIRES or structured extraction from text may also be valuable for comparison.

*   **Potential Influence:** CDE-Mapper offers a pragmatic approach to clinical data standardization. If the computational costs can be managed and the knowledge base effectively curated, it could have a positive impact on clinical research and decision support.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel application of RAG to a practical problem in healthcare informatics. The results show statistically significant improvements in the concept linking, with reasonable ablation analysis. However, there remain issues in terms of the computational cost, potential knowledge base dependency, and some limited comparison to state-of-the-art methods. CDE-Mapper has the potential to be significant because clinical data interoperability remains important to the medical community.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Balancing Accuracy, Calibration, and Efficiency in Active Learning with Vision Transformers Under Label Noise
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04375v1)
- **Authors**: Moseli Mots'oehli, Hope Mogale, Kyungim Baek
- **Abstract**: Fine-tuning pre-trained convolutional neural networks on ImageNet for downstream tasks is well-established. Still, the impact of model size on the performance of vision transformers in similar scenarios, particularly under label noise, remains largely unexplored. Given the utility and versatility of transformer architectures, this study investigates their practicality under low-budget constraints and noisy labels. We explore how classification accuracy and calibration are affected by symmetric label noise in active learning settings, evaluating four vision transformer configurations (Base and Large with 16x16 and 32x32 patch sizes) and three Swin Transformer configurations (Tiny, Small, and Base) on CIFAR10 and CIFAR100 datasets, under varying label noise rates. Our findings show that larger ViT models (ViTl32 in particular) consistently outperform their smaller counterparts in both accuracy and calibration, even under moderate to high label noise, while Swin Transformers exhibit weaker robustness across all noise levels. We find that smaller patch sizes do not always lead to better performance, as ViTl16 performs consistently worse than ViTl32 while incurring a higher computational cost. We also find that information-based Active Learning strategies only provide meaningful accuracy improvements at moderate label noise rates, but they result in poorer calibration compared to models trained on randomly acquired labels, especially at high label noise rates. We hope these insights provide actionable guidance for practitioners looking to deploy vision transformers in resource-constrained environments, where balancing model complexity, label noise, and compute efficiency is critical in model fine-tuning or distillation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the performance of Vision Transformers (ViTs) and Swin Transformers in active learning (AL) scenarios under label noise. It examines how different model sizes, patch sizes, and embedding dimensions impact classification accuracy and calibration on CIFAR10 and CIFAR100 datasets with varying levels of symmetric label noise. The study compares the performance of different ViT and Swin Transformer architectures using three active learning strategies: random sampling, entropy sampling, and GCI_ViTAL. The key findings indicate that larger ViT models (especially ViTl32) generally outperform smaller counterparts and Swin Transformers in both accuracy and calibration, even under moderate to high label noise. Additionally, it reveals that information-based AL strategies primarily offer accuracy improvements at moderate label noise rates but can lead to poorer calibration, particularly at higher noise levels. The paper provides practical guidance for selecting transformer models in resource-constrained environments with label noise, emphasizing the importance of balancing model complexity, noise levels, and computational efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its systematic exploration of ViT architectures, patch sizes, and embeddings within the context of active learning *under label noise*. While individual aspects have been explored, the comprehensive examination of these interacting factors is a valuable contribution. It builds on existing work comparing ViTs and CNNs in DAL by directly addressing model complexity in the DAL setting.
*   **Significance:** The study addresses a practical concern in real-world machine learning, where data labeling is often imperfect and resources are limited. By providing insights into the behavior of ViTs and Swin Transformers under label noise in AL scenarios, the paper helps practitioners make informed decisions about model selection and training strategies. The findings suggest that simply defaulting to large models might not be optimal under all conditions, highlighting the importance of data- and situation-specific solutions. This is a significant addition. The emphasis on calibration alongside accuracy is another valuable aspect, as well-calibrated models are crucial in applications where reliable uncertainty estimates are needed.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Experimentation:** The paper conducts a thorough set of experiments, covering various model sizes, patch sizes, label noise rates, and active learning strategies.
    *   **Focus on Practical Considerations:** The study highlights the importance of balancing accuracy, calibration, and computational efficiency in resource-constrained environments.
    *   **Clear Presentation of Results:** The results are presented clearly and concisely, with informative tables and figures.
    *   **Directly addresses the research question:** The question is whether larger ViTs are more robust under label noise for active learning.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited DAL Strategies:** The paper uses only three DAL strategies, limiting the breadth of the study. While the authors justify this choice by focusing on model characteristics, exploring a wider range of strategies could have provided further insights.
    *   **Specific Datasets:** The study relies on CIFAR10 and CIFAR100, which are relatively simple datasets. While commonly used, extending the analysis to more complex and diverse datasets would enhance the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Limited architectural details:** Given how critical hyperparameters in vision transformers can be, a deeper discussion of how the patch size and embedding dimensions influence feature extraction is missing.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the selection and deployment of ViTs in active learning scenarios, particularly in domains where label noise and limited resources are prevalent. It emphasizes the need for a balanced approach, considering not only accuracy but also calibration and computational efficiency. Future research could build upon this work by exploring more robust DAL strategies that are specifically tailored to ViTs under label noise.
*   **Rigorous rationale for score assigned:** While the work is solid and addresses a relevant problem, the limited range of DAL strategies and the reliance on standard datasets moderately restricts the potential impact on the broader field of active learning. The strong experimental design and the actionable recommendations for practitioners in label-constrained environments are strong. This combined with the exploration of various architecture sizes under various noise rates leads to a middle ground score.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### The Aloe Family Recipe for Open and Specialized Healthcare LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04388v1)
- **Authors**: Dario Garcia-Gasulla, Jordi Bayarri-Planas, Ashwin Kumar Gururajan, Enrique Lopez-Cuena, Adrian Tormos, Daniel Hinjos, Pablo Bernabeu-Perez, Anna Arias-Duart, Pablo Agustin Martin-Torres, Marta Gonzalez-Mallo, Sergio Alvarez-Napagao, Eduard Ayguadé-Parra, Ulises Cortés
- **Abstract**: Purpose: With advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) for healthcare, the need arises for competitive open-source models to protect the public interest. This work contributes to the field of open medical LLMs by optimizing key stages of data preprocessing and training, while showing how to improve model safety (through DPO) and efficacy (through RAG). The evaluation methodology used, which includes four different types of tests, defines a new standard for the field. The resultant models, shown to be competitive with the best private alternatives, are released with a permisive license. Methods: Building on top of strong base models like Llama 3.1 and Qwen 2.5, Aloe Beta uses a custom dataset to enhance public data with synthetic Chain of Thought examples. The models undergo alignment with Direct Preference Optimization, emphasizing ethical and policy-aligned performance in the presence of jailbreaking attacks. Evaluation includes close-ended, open-ended, safety and human assessments, to maximize the reliability of results. Results: Recommendations are made across the entire pipeline, backed by the solid performance of the Aloe Family. These models deliver competitive performance across healthcare benchmarks and medical fields, and are often preferred by healthcare professionals. On bias and toxicity, the Aloe Beta models significantly improve safety, showing resilience to unseen jailbreaking attacks. For a responsible release, a detailed risk assessment specific to healthcare is attached to the Aloe Family models. Conclusion: The Aloe Beta models, and the recipe that leads to them, are a significant contribution to the open-source medical LLM field, offering top-of-the-line performance while maintaining high ethical requirements. This work sets a new standard for developing and reporting aligned LLMs in healthcare.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper "The Aloe Family Recipe for Open and Specialized Healthcare LLMs" presents a comprehensive approach to building and evaluating open-source Large Language Models (LLMs) specifically for the healthcare domain. The authors focus on optimizing key stages of data preprocessing and training, and they also demonstrate how to enhance model safety through Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) and efficacy using Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). The paper emphasizes the importance of open-source models to ensure public access and benefit from advancements in healthcare LLMs. Key contributions include a novel training methodology (the "Aloe Recipe"), a custom dataset, and a rigorous evaluation framework that includes closed-ended, open-ended, safety, and human assessments. The resulting Aloe Family models are released with a permissive license and a detailed risk assessment for healthcare applications.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

This paper makes a solid contribution to the rapidly evolving field of open-source healthcare LLMs. The authors address a critical need for transparent, reproducible, and ethically sound models in a domain with sensitive applications. While many individual components of their approach have been explored before (e.g., fine-tuning, model merging, RAG), the paper's novelty lies in the *integrated and optimized* application of these techniques within a well-defined pipeline for healthcare LLMs, combined with a *new evaluation methodology*.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper covers the entire lifecycle of LLM development, from data curation to training to evaluation and deployment.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The "Aloe Recipe" is well-defined, and the authors provide detailed descriptions of their data preprocessing, training stages, and evaluation metrics.
*   **Focus on Safety and Ethics:** The incorporation of DPO for model alignment and the inclusion of a detailed risk assessment demonstrate a strong commitment to responsible AI development.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The use of a variety of evaluation methods (MCQA, open-ended, human, and safety) increases the reliability of the results and provides a more complete picture of model performance.
*   **Open Source Contribution:** Releasing the models, datasets, and code promotes reproducibility and allows other researchers to build upon their work.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The Aloe models achieve competitive performance on healthcare benchmarks and are often preferred by healthcare professionals, demonstrating the effectiveness of the "Aloe Recipe." The results on reducing jailbreaking are especially noteworthy.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the *combination* of techniques is novel, the individual techniques themselves (fine-tuning, model merging, RAG, DPO) are well-established. The paper could benefit from a more in-depth discussion of how their specific implementation and parameter choices significantly improve upon existing approaches.
*   **Limited Depth on Some Evaluation Aspects:** Although the evaluation framework is comprehensive, some aspects, such as the open-ended evaluation, rely on metrics like ROUGE that have known limitations. The paper acknowledges these limitations but could suggest alternative or complementary metrics for future work.
*   **Modest Gains vs. Base Models on Llama3.1:** The performance improvements over the base Llama 3.1 models are sometimes marginal in MCQA settings. While Qwen 2.5 shows larger gains, it is important to acknowledge and discuss the relatively smaller improvements on some of the metrics when using the stronger base model.
*   **Reliance on Reddit Data for Human Evaluation:** While the authors took measures to anonymize the Reddit data used for human evaluation, it still introduces a potential bias and limits the generalizability of the findings. Medical questions gathered are probably too easy.

**Potential Influence on the Field:**

*   The "Aloe Recipe" could serve as a valuable starting point for other researchers developing open-source healthcare LLMs, providing a proven methodology for achieving competitive performance and improved safety.
*   The comprehensive evaluation framework could become a standard for assessing and reporting aligned LLMs in healthcare, promoting greater transparency and comparability across different models.
*   The paper's emphasis on ethical considerations could encourage more responsible AI development practices in the healthcare domain, leading to more trustworthy and beneficial LLMs for clinical applications.
*   The release of high-quality, permissively licensed healthcare LLMs could democratize access to these technologies, enabling smaller research groups and healthcare organizations to leverage the power of LLMs without relying on proprietary solutions.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign this paper a score of **7.5**.  The work makes a solid and valuable contribution by providing a well-defined recipe for building and evaluating open-source healthcare LLMs. While the individual components are not entirely new, the integrated and optimized application, combined with the focus on safety and a comprehensive evaluation framework, makes it a noteworthy addition to the field. The experimental results are promising, and the open-source release ensures broad accessibility and potential impact. The weaknesses related to incremental novelty and limitations in the open-ended and human evaluation prevented a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### Large Means Left: Political Bias in Large Language Models Increases with Their Number of Parameters
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04393v1)
- **Authors**: David Exler, Mark Schutera, Markus Reischl, Luca Rettenberger
- **Abstract**: With the increasing prevalence of artificial intelligence, careful evaluation of inherent biases needs to be conducted to form the basis for alleviating the effects these predispositions can have on users. Large language models (LLMs) are predominantly used by many as a primary source of information for various topics. LLMs frequently make factual errors, fabricate data (hallucinations), or present biases, exposing users to misinformation and influencing opinions. Educating users on their risks is key to responsible use, as bias, unlike hallucinations, cannot be caught through data verification. We quantify the political bias of popular LLMs in the context of the recent vote of the German Bundestag using the score produced by the Wahl-O-Mat. This metric measures the alignment between an individual's political views and the positions of German political parties. We compare the models' alignment scores to identify factors influencing their political preferences. Doing so, we discover a bias toward left-leaning parties, most dominant in larger LLMs. Also, we find that the language we use to communicate with the models affects their political views. Additionally, we analyze the influence of a model's origin and release date and compare the results to the outcome of the recent vote of the Bundestag. Our results imply that LLMs are prone to exhibiting political bias. Large corporations with the necessary means to develop LLMs, thus, knowingly or unknowingly, have a responsibility to contain these biases, as they can influence each voter's decision-making process and inform public opinion in general and at scale.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, with an emphasis on a rigorous and justified scoring:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the political biases of Large Language Models (LLMs) in the context of the German Bundestag. It uses the "Wahl-O-Mat," a tool for gauging political alignment, to assess how LLMs respond to political statements. The study analyzes several factors, including model size, release date, and language of input, to determine their influence on political preferences. The key findings suggest a tendency towards left-leaning biases in LLMs, with larger models exhibiting this bias more prominently. The language of input also affects the LLM's political view. The study concludes that corporations developing LLMs have a responsibility to mitigate these biases due to their potential to influence public opinion.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relevance:** The topic of political bias in LLMs is highly relevant given the increasing use of these models as sources of information.
    *   **Methodology:** The use of the Wahl-O-Mat provides a structured and quantifiable way to assess political alignment, which improves the rigor of the analysis compared to solely qualitative assessments. The methodology is mostly appropriate with exception of using a linear seat allocation for finding the left/right positioning score. This means may not be appropriate due to the non-linear nature of political positioning.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper considers several potential factors influencing bias (model size, release date, language), offering a multi-faceted perspective.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The use of open-source models and a well-defined methodology makes the study potentially reproducible, which is crucial for validating the findings.
    *   **Comparison to prior work:** It provides good comparision with prior work, most notably Rettenberger, Reischl, and Schutera (2025).
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses primarily on the German political context and Bundestag. While this allows for a focused analysis, it limits the generalizability of the findings to other political systems and countries.
    *   **Causality Concerns:** While the paper identifies correlations between model size, language, and political bias, it doesn't establish clear causal relationships. The source of the bias (e.g., training data, model architecture) remains somewhat speculative.
    *   **Neutral Statements:** The interpretation of neutral statements as potentially leaning a specific way weakens the analysis.
    *   **Influence on Voters:** The paper states it is unlikely that the bias had any effect on the vote, yet no reasoning for this statement is provided.
    *   **Linear Seat Allocation:** The use of a linear seat allocation may not be an accurate depiction of the political positioning in the Bundestag. This could weaken the analysis on the position of LLMs in the political spectrum.
*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper adds to the growing body of literature on bias in LLMs, specifically focusing on the under-explored area of political biases. While prior work has investigated similar biases, this paper extends the analysis by considering factors like release date, model origin (specifically, a Chinese model), and newer models (DeepSeek R1, SimpleScaling S1).
    *   The use of the Wahl-O-Mat provides a unique framework for quantifying political alignment within a specific national context.
    *   The finding that larger models exhibit a stronger left-leaning bias is significant, as it raises concerns about the potential for increasingly influential LLMs to amplify existing biases.

    Overall, the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by providing further evidence and a more detailed analysis of political bias in LLMs. It strengthens the case for ongoing scrutiny and transparency in LLM development. However, the limited scope, causality concerns, and challenges in interpreting results prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution.

**Score: 6.5**

**Justification:**

I am assigning a score of 6.5 because:

*   The paper's topic is timely and important (positive factor).
*   The method is mostly appropriate, but with the caveat of questionable linear seat allocation. (mixed factor)
*   The study expands on previous research by analyzing new factors, LLMs, and a specific national context, providing incremental rather than transformative insights (moderate positive factor).
*   The causality and scope limitations (generalizability to different political spectrums), as well as the influence on voters weakens the impact of the conclusion (negative factors).

While the paper provides valuable evidence of political bias in LLMs and explores potentially influencing factors, its limited scope, questions on the validity of the positioning methodology, and its inability to establish causality prevent it from being considered a highly novel or impactful contribution to the field. It is a solid and worthwhile addition to the literature, but it doesn't represent a significant paradigm shift in understanding or mitigating bias in LLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### YABLoCo: Yet Another Benchmark for Long Context Code Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04406v1)
- **Authors**: Aidar Valeev, Roman Garaev, Vadim Lomshakov, Irina Piontkovskaya, Vladimir Ivanov, Israel Adewuyi
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models demonstrate the ability to solve various programming tasks, including code generation. Typically, the performance of LLMs is measured on benchmarks with small or medium-sized context windows of thousands of lines of code. At the same time, in real-world software projects, repositories can span up to millions of LoC. This paper closes this gap by contributing to the long context code generation benchmark (YABLoCo). The benchmark featured a test set of 215 functions selected from four large repositories with thousands of functions. The dataset contained metadata of functions, contexts of the functions with different levels of dependencies, docstrings, functions bodies, and call graphs for each repository. This paper presents three key aspects of the contribution. First, the benchmark aims at function body generation in large repositories in C and C++, two languages not covered by previous benchmarks. Second, the benchmark contains large repositories from 200K to 2,000K LoC. Third, we contribute a scalable evaluation pipeline for efficient computing of the target metrics and a tool for visual analysis of generated code. Overall, these three aspects allow for evaluating code generation in large repositories in C and C++.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "YABLoCo: Yet Another Benchmark for Long Context Code Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces YABLoCo, a new benchmark for evaluating the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in generating code within the context of large software repositories.  Unlike existing benchmarks that primarily focus on smaller code snippets or Python/Java, YABLoCo is designed for C/C++ repositories containing hundreds of thousands to millions of lines of code.  The benchmark includes 215 functions extracted from four large, real-world C/C++ projects (llvm, bullet3, openssl, redis), along with their function bodies, docstrings, signatures, function call graphs, and other metadata. The authors also provide an open-source evaluation pipeline and a tool for visual analysis of generated code.  The paper presents baseline results using CodeLlama, DeepSeek Coder, and GPT-4, examining performance with and without "oracle" context (functions called within target function).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a reasonable level of novelty. While repository-level code generation benchmarks exist, YABLoCo distinguishes itself through its focus on C/C++, its inclusion of genuinely large repositories (200K-2000K LoC), and its explicit consideration of function call dependencies at different levels (file, package, project). The addition of function call graphs is a valuable contribution, enriching the contextual information available to LLMs. The visualization tool enhances usability. The choice of C/C++ is commendable, addressing a gap in the existing benchmark landscape.

*   **Significance:** The YABLoCo benchmark has the potential to be significant for the code generation community, especially those working with C/C++. Evaluating LLMs on real-world, large-scale C/C++ projects is crucial for understanding their limitations and capabilities in realistic software engineering scenarios. The availability of the dataset and evaluation pipeline should facilitate further research and development in this area. The baseline results, while not groundbreaking, offer a starting point for future comparisons. Addressing the long-context challenge in code generation is important, and this benchmark directly targets that issue.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Focus on C/C++ and Large Repositories:** Addresses a gap in the existing benchmark landscape.
    *   **Contextual Information:** Includes function call graphs, enabling better understanding of dependencies.
    *   **Open-Source Evaluation Pipeline:** Facilitates reproducibility and further research.
    *   **Visual Analysis Tool:** Aids in understanding model behavior and identifying areas for improvement.
    *   **Careful Filtering:**  The method for data collection and cleaning using static analysis is clearly outlined.
    *  **Thorough description:** The methodology behind the filtering process used on the data is thorough and well described.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Number of Repositories and Functions:** While large, the benchmark includes only four repositories and 215 functions. A larger and more diverse dataset would increase its generalizability.
    *   **Potential Data Leakage:** Two of the repositories (llvm, bullet3) are present in Stack, a common pre-training dataset. The paper acknowledges this but doesn't fully address its potential impact. Obfuscation of signatures is proposed as a mitigation strategy, but a more robust approach might be necessary.
    *   **Simplified Prompting Strategy:**  The "oracle" context approach is a simple way to incorporate context, but it may not be the most effective.  More sophisticated retrieval and context integration methods could be explored.
    *   **Dependency on Existing Tests:** Reliance on existing test suites may not adequately assess the correctness of generated code. The paper acknowledges this limitation, but new tests would increase the reliability of benchmark.
    *  **Limited evaluation:** There are only three metrics used when evaluating code quality: Pass@k, Exact Match, and Edit Similarity.
    * **Missing comparison**: The dataset could have benefitted from including previous datasets for comparison.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper presents a valuable contribution by introducing a new benchmark tailored for long-context code generation in C/C++ repositories. However, the limitations related to dataset size, potential data leakage, simplified prompting strategy, and reliance on existing tests prevent it from achieving a higher score. While the benchmark has the potential to be influential, improvements are needed to address these weaknesses and enhance its robustness and generalizability. A benchmark is an important contribution, but it lacks in depth of evalution, and dataset diversity.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### OBLIVIATE: Robust and Practical Machine Unlearning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04416v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Xu, Minxin Du, Qingqing Ye, Haibo Hu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) trained over extensive corpora risk memorizing sensitive, copyrighted, or toxic content. To address this, we propose OBLIVIATE, a robust unlearning framework that removes targeted data while preserving model utility. The framework follows a structured process: extracting target tokens, building retain sets, and fine-tuning with a tailored loss function comprising three components -- masking, distillation, and world fact. Using low-rank adapters (LoRA), it ensures efficiency without compromising unlearning quality. We conduct experiments on multiple datasets, including the Harry Potter series, WMDP, and TOFU, using a comprehensive suite of metrics: forget quality (new document-level memorization score), model utility, and fluency. Results demonstrate its effectiveness in resisting membership inference attacks, minimizing the impact on retained data, and maintaining robustness across diverse scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "OBLIVIATE: Robust and Practical Machine Unlearning for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OBLIVIATE, a machine unlearning framework for LLMs designed to remove targeted data effectively while preserving model utility and fluency. The framework involves extracting target tokens, building retain sets, and fine-tuning with a tailored loss function comprising masking, distillation, and world fact components.  Low-rank adapters (LoRA) are used for efficiency. The method is evaluated on multiple datasets including the Harry Potter series, WMDP, and TOFU, using various metrics such as forget quality, model utility, and fluency.  The results suggest OBLIVIATE is effective in resisting membership inference attacks, minimizing impact on retained data, and maintaining robustness across diverse scenarios.  A new metric, "document-level memorization" (DRMA), is introduced to capture broader memorization behavior.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Practicality Focus:** The paper emphasizes the practicality of the method, which is vital in the unlearning domain, addressing challenges like computational cost and retaining model utility. The use of LoRA is a key factor here.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation considers forget quality, model utility, and fluency, providing a well-rounded view of the framework's performance. The experiments are performed across multiple datasets, each with varying challenges (copyrighted content, harmful outputs, synthetic data).
    *   **Novelty in Loss Function:** The structured loss function, with masking, distillation, and world fact components, is a significant contribution. It tackles the challenges of aggressive forgetting and catastrophic collapse. The context-aware unlearning capabilities are particularly promising.
    *   **DRMA Metric:** The introduction of document-level memorization (DRMA) is a valuable addition to evaluation metrics. It helps to capture broader patterns and assess the overall memorization behavior of LLMs, particularly important for multi-sequence content.
    *   **Use of GPT-4 for Token Identification**: The use of GPT-4 for identifying target tokens is a pragmatic approach, leveraging the capabilities of a powerful model to improve the unlearning process.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on GPT-4**: While the use of GPT-4 for token identification is beneficial, it introduces a reliance on an external model and may introduce retrieval instability. The authors acknowledge this limitation. Furthermore, there are costs and challenges associated with using such a model, including subtle, context-dependent expressions.
    *   **Limited Model Scale in Experiments:** The largest model used in the evaluation is Llama3-8B-Instruct. While valuable, the results might not directly translate to much larger, state-of-the-art models. Evaluating on larger models, such as Llama 2 or GPT-3 class, would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Fluency Issues:** The paper acknowledges that generated outputs sometimes contain gibberish or are blank, indicating a trade-off between forget quality and fluency. This is a point that requires further refinement.
    *   **GPT-4 Evaluation:** Though using GPT-4 for fluency evaluation can be efficient, it might have biases or not align with human judgment in certain scenarios.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis**: The paper relies primarily on empirical evaluation. While this is common in LLM unlearning, a theoretical analysis of the framework's properties (e.g., relating to differential privacy or convergence) would further solidify the contribution.
    *   **Some Experiments on Smaller Datasets**: the TOFU-forget01 and -forget05 datasets showed limited unlearning capabilities. The unlearning might need some modification for these smaller sets.

*   **Significance and Potential Influence:**

    The paper addresses a crucial problem in the LLM field: the ethical and legal risks associated with memorized sensitive or copyrighted content. OBLIVIATE offers a practical and well-evaluated framework that can have a real impact on how LLMs are deployed and managed. The emphasis on model utility, fluency, and efficiency makes it a promising solution for real-world applications. By balancing aggressiveness, the method resists member inference attacks, minimizes impact on retained data and maintains a balance between model utility and fluency. It also avoids the use of negative updates, which can have many negative consequences for forgetting.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper has some limitations, its strengths outweigh them. The focus on practicality, comprehensive evaluation, and the novel loss function design make OBLIVIATE a valuable contribution to the LLM unlearning field. The limitations, such as the dependence on GPT-4 for token selection and the limited scale of evaluated models, are acknowledged by the authors and provide clear directions for future research.

Score: 8

*Rationale*: The score reflects the good balance between the paper's strengths and weaknesses. The paper shows a solid improvement to existing unlearning techniques, but can have more improvements in scalability to bigger models and some evaluation metrics.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Localized Diffusion Models for High Dimensional Distributions Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04417v1)
- **Authors**: Georg A. Gottwald, Shuigen Liu, Youssef Marzouk, Sebastian Reich, Xin T. Tong
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models are the state-of-the-art tools for various generative tasks. However, estimating high-dimensional score functions makes them potentially suffer from the curse of dimensionality (CoD). This underscores the importance of better understanding and exploiting low-dimensional structure in the target distribution. In this work, we consider locality structure, which describes sparse dependencies between model components. Under locality structure, the score function is effectively low-dimensional, so that it can be estimated by a localized neural network with significantly reduced sample complexity. This motivates the localized diffusion model, where a localized score matching loss is used to train the score function within a localized hypothesis space. We prove that such localization enables diffusion models to circumvent CoD, at the price of additional localization error. Under realistic sample size scaling, we show both theoretically and numerically that a moderate localization radius can balance the statistical and localization error, leading to a better overall performance. The localized structure also facilitates parallel training of diffusion models, making it potentially more efficient for large-scale applications.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper, "Localized Diffusion Models for High Dimensional Distributions Generation." Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the curse of dimensionality (CoD) in diffusion models (DMs) when dealing with high-dimensional data. The authors propose a localized diffusion model (LDM) that leverages the locality structure, which describes sparse dependencies between model components. They argue that under locality structure, the score function (a crucial component of DMs) is effectively low-dimensional and can be estimated by a localized neural network with reduced sample complexity. The paper provides theoretical justifications, including an approximation and generalization analysis demonstrating how localization can circumvent CoD at the cost of localization error. Numerical experiments on high-dimensional time series data are presented to validate the theoretical results, suggesting that a moderate localization radius balances statistical and localization error. Finally, the paper claims that LDM facilitates parallel training, potentially improving efficiency for large-scale applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of exploiting locality structure to mitigate the CoD in generative models is not entirely novel. The manifold hypothesis and Gaussian mixture models have previously been explored for this purpose. Similarly, localized sampling techniques have been explored in the context of MCMC and Schrodinger Bridges. However, the application of locality structure *specifically within the context of diffusion models* and the formal analysis of the trade-off between statistical error and localization error *is* a more novel contribution.

*   **Significance:** If the theoretical results and the empirical evidence hold up under further scrutiny, this work could have a significant impact. DMs have shown remarkable performance, but their applicability to truly high-dimensional problems is limited by the CoD. By providing a framework for incorporating locality structure, the LDM potentially extends the reach of DMs to a broader class of problems, particularly those with inherent sparsity or localized dependencies (e.g., spatial models, time series, certain types of graphical models). The claim that LDMs facilitate parallel training is another potentially significant benefit, as it could address the computational cost associated with training DMs on large datasets.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies a significant limitation of DMs (CoD) and proposes a well-motivated approach.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The approximation and generalization analysis provides a solid foundation for the LDM. It provides some theoretical backing for the claimed benefits of localization in balancing statistical and localization errors.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The numerical experiments on time series data provide preliminary support for the theoretical results. The paper does include interesting validation regarding the importance of appropriate localization.
    *   **Potential for Parallel Training:** The decomposition of the score function, enabling parallel training, is a notable advantage.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Assumptions:** The theoretical analysis relies on assumptions such as log-concavity and smoothness of the target distribution, which may not hold in all practical scenarios. The (S,v) locality condition is also restrictive and may not hold. The paper should more clearly address the limitations of the LDM under these assumptions.
    *   **Limited Empirical Evaluation:** The empirical validation is limited to one type of high-dimensional time series data. More extensive experiments on other types of data (e.g., image datasets with known locality structures) are needed to demonstrate the broader applicability of the LDM.
    *   **Practical Implementation:** The paper acknowledges that determining appropriate locality structure requires prior knowledge, although this can be learned. The algorithm for learning the structure adaptively is not explicitly described in the paper. This needs to be described.
    *   **Comparison to other COD methods:** The paper should compare LDMs to other approaches for avoiding the CoD in diffusion models in more detail.

*   **Impact:** If the framework stands up to further scrutiny, then the paper could have substantial impact. It provides a good basis for further work that expands LDMs to a wider range of problems.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach (localized DMs), grounded in theoretical analysis and supported by initial empirical results. The novelty lies in specifically addressing CoD in DMs by embedding locality structure directly within the model. The results hold the potential to meaningfully extend the applicability of diffusion models in settings where low-dimensional structure is present. However, the paper's reliance on specific assumptions, limited empirical validation, and absence of a comparison with existing alternatives weaken its significance. These assumptions and limitations diminish the potential impact of the paper to some extent, warranting a cautious assessment. Further validation, broader empirical testing, and analysis of the adaptability to different scenarios are needed to fully assess its contributions and impact.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### LONGER: Scaling Up Long Sequence Modeling in Industrial Recommenders
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04421v1)
- **Authors**: Zheng Chai, Qin Ren, Xijun Xiao, Huizhi Yang, Bo Han, Sijun Zhang, Di Chen, Hui Lu, Wenlin Zhao, Lele Yu, Xionghang Xie, Shiru Ren, Xiang Sun, Yaocheng Tan, Peng Xu, Yuchao Zheng, Di Wu
- **Abstract**: Modeling ultra-long user behavior sequences is critical for capturing both long- and short-term preferences in industrial recommender systems. Existing solutions typically rely on two-stage retrieval or indirect modeling paradigms, incuring upstream-downstream inconsistency and computational inefficiency. In this paper, we present LONGER, a Long-sequence Optimized traNsformer for GPU-Efficient Recommenders. LONGER incorporates (i) a global token mechanism for stabilizing attention over long contexts, (ii) a token merge module with lightweight InnerTransformers and hybrid attention strategy to reduce quadratic complexity, and (iii) a series of engineering optimizations, including training with mixed-precision and activation recomputation, KV cache serving, and the fully synchronous model training and serving framework for unified GPU-based dense and sparse parameter updates. LONGER consistently outperforms strong baselines in both offline metrics and online A/B testing in both advertising and e-commerce services at ByteDance, validating its consistent effectiveness and industrial-level scaling laws. Currently, LONGER has been fully deployed at more than 10 influential scenarios at ByteDance, serving billion users.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LONGER: Scaling Up Long Sequence Modeling in Industrial Recommenders":

**Summary:**

The paper presents LONGER, a novel Transformer-based architecture optimized for modeling ultra-long user behavior sequences in industrial recommender systems.  The architecture addresses the computational challenges associated with long sequences through several key innovations:  (1) the use of global tokens to stabilize attention and facilitate global information fusion; (2) a token merge module with InnerTransformers and hybrid attention to reduce quadratic complexity; and (3) engineering optimizations for training and serving, including mixed-precision training, KV caching, and a fully synchronous training framework. The authors demonstrate LONGER's effectiveness and efficiency through offline experiments on a large-scale industrial dataset and online A/B tests on two influential services at ByteDance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper combines several existing techniques in a novel and practical way for industrial-scale long-sequence modeling.
