While the individual components, such as global tokens or token merging, might have precedents in other domains (e.g., language modeling), their integration and optimization specifically for recommender systems and within the constraints of a large-scale industrial setting represents a meaningful contribution.  The focus on GPU-efficient training and serving, incorporating mixed precision, recomputation, and KV caching, demonstrates a clear understanding of the engineering challenges involved. The specific combination of token merge and lightweight InnerTransformers to retain local semantics adds to the novelty. The hybrid attention mechanism is also a thoughtful design choice, balancing global context with local interactions.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in recommender systems: effectively modeling long-term user behavior for improved recommendations. The scaling laws are presented and validated through online A/B tests, suggesting that it can meaningfully influence industry practice. The deployment of LONGER in over 10 scenarios at ByteDance, impacting billions of users, speaks volumes about its practical relevance and significance. The paper's strength lies in its demonstrated ability to bridge the gap between research and real-world implementation. The details provided on the training and serving infrastructure are valuable for researchers and practitioners looking to adopt similar techniques.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Focus:**  The paper is strongly motivated by real-world industrial applications and addresses the unique challenges of deploying long-sequence models in such environments.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper provides a thorough evaluation with both offline and online experiments, demonstrating the effectiveness and efficiency of LONGER.
    *   **Detailed Engineering Optimizations:** The paper includes a detailed description of the engineering optimizations used to make LONGER scalable and efficient.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition and Solution:** The paper clearly defines the problem of long-sequence modeling in recommender systems and provides a well-structured and effective solution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Innovation:**  While the combination of techniques is novel, some of the individual components are not groundbreaking. The paper could benefit from a more in-depth comparison to other long-sequence modeling techniques in the recommender systems literature, particularly those using memory networks or hierarchical attention mechanisms, especially discussing tradeoffs regarding performance, memory, and latency.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:**  The paper lacks a rigorous theoretical analysis of the proposed architecture. It could benefit from discussing the complexity bounds of the attention mechanism and the potential for generalization.
    *  **Dataset details:** Information about the data processing done to generate the dataset or the features used is limited.

**Justification of Score:**

While the individual components of LONGER may not be entirely novel, the paper demonstrates a strong ability to combine these components, optimizing them for a specific and important industrial application. The practical focus, comprehensive evaluation, and detailed engineering optimizations make this paper a valuable contribution to the field of recommender systems. The demonstrated impact on billions of users further reinforces its significance. The weaknesses are also a part of research and can be improved in the future.

### Theoretical Guarantees for LT-TTD: A Unified Transformer-based Architecture for Two-Level Ranking Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04434v1)
- **Authors**: Ayoub Abraich
- **Abstract**: Modern recommendation and search systems typically employ multi-stage ranking architectures to efficiently handle billions of candidates. The conventional approach uses distinct L1 (candidate retrieval) and L2 (re-ranking) models with different optimization objectives, introducing critical limitations including irreversible error propagation and suboptimal ranking. This paper identifies and analyzes the fundamental limitations of this decoupled paradigm and proposes LT-TTD (Listwise Transformer with Two-Tower Distillation), a novel unified architecture that bridges retrieval and ranking phases. Our approach combines the computational efficiency of two-tower models with the expressivity of transformers in a unified listwise learning framework. We provide a comprehensive theoretical analysis of our architecture and establish formal guarantees regarding error propagation mitigation, ranking quality improvements, and optimization convergence. We derive theoretical bounds showing that LT-TTD reduces the upper limit on irretrievable relevant items by a factor that depends on the knowledge distillation strength, and prove that our multi-objective optimization framework achieves a provably better global optimum than disjoint training. Additionally, we analyze the computational complexity of our approach, demonstrating that the asymptotic complexity remains within practical bounds for real-world applications. We also introduce UPQE, a novel evaluation metric specifically designed for unified ranking architectures that holistically captures retrieval quality, ranking performance, and computational efficiency.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the document. Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LT-TTD (Listwise Transformer with Two-Tower Distillation), a new unified architecture for large-scale ranking systems designed to overcome the limitations of traditional two-level cascade ranking systems (L1 retrieval followed by L2 re-ranking). LT-TTD combines the efficiency of two-tower models with the expressiveness of Transformers in a listwise learning framework. The key components are: (1) a Two-Tower Encoder (TTE) for fast similarity-based retrieval, (2) a Listwise Transformer (LT) that processes candidate lists to capture cross-item interactions, and (3) a Knowledge Distillation Bridge (KDB) for bidirectional knowledge transfer between the TTE and LT components.  The paper presents a multi-objective optimization framework for training LT-TTD, theoretical guarantees regarding error propagation reduction, ranking quality improvements, optimization convergence, and computational complexity. Finally, it proposes a novel evaluation metric, UPQE (Unified Propagation-aware Quality Efficiency), specifically designed for unified ranking architectures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   The key novelty lies in unifying the retrieval and ranking stages into a single architecture. Traditional cascade systems have long been plagued by issues of error propagation and conflicting optimization objectives. LT-TTD attempts to address these problems by creating a bidirectional flow of information between the two stages using knowledge distillation.
*   The use of a Listwise Transformer to capture cross-item interactions in a context where two-tower models are traditionally used is a significant contribution. The knowledge distillation bridge (KDB) is also a novel element that aims to align the objectives of the computationally efficient TTE and the more expressive LT.
*   Introducing the UPQE metric is novel, as it addresses the limitations of standard ranking metrics when evaluating such architectures.

**Significance:**

*   If successful, LT-TTD could significantly improve the ranking performance of large-scale recommendation and search systems. The ability to mitigate error propagation and jointly optimize retrieval and ranking has the potential to lead to more relevant and personalized results.
*   The theoretical analysis provides a strong foundation for the approach, offering guarantees regarding error propagation reduction, ranking quality, and convergence. This is crucial for demonstrating the viability of the method and providing insights for practical implementation.
*   The UPQE metric could become a valuable tool for evaluating unified ranking architectures, facilitating the development of more effective and efficient systems.

**Strengths:**

*   The paper clearly identifies and formalizes the limitations of traditional cascade ranking systems.
*   The LT-TTD architecture is well-motivated and addresses the identified limitations in a principled manner.
*   The theoretical analysis is comprehensive and provides valuable insights into the behavior of the model.
*   The UPQE metric is a useful addition to the evaluation toolkit for unified ranking architectures.

**Weaknesses:**

*   The paper is purely theoretical. There are no experimental results to validate the claims made in the paper. The theoretical guarantees are only as good as the assumptions made, and experimental validation is needed to determine how well the model performs in practice.
*   The paper assumes the model will be implementable without huge complexity and latency that could be induced by transformers. This assumption is not necessarily validated.

**Potential Impact:**

*   If the model works well, the impact could be high and could change the way information retrieval is implemented in real life.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-reasoned architecture for unified ranking. However, the lack of experimental validation is a major drawback. While the theoretical analysis provides valuable insights, it does not guarantee that the model will perform as expected in real-world scenarios. The practicality of the architecture is also questionable. For these reasons, the potential has not been demonstrated at this point. It has provided a very theoretical method, that still needs to be implemented and tested.

Score: 6

### Towards Effectively Leveraging Execution Traces for Program Repair with Code LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04441v1)
- **Authors**: Mirazul Haque, Petr Babkin, Farima Farmahinifarahani, Manuela Veloso
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) show promising performance on various programming tasks, including Automatic Program Repair (APR). However, most approaches to LLM-based APR are limited to the static analysis of the programs, while disregarding their runtime behavior. Inspired by knowledge-augmented NLP, in this work, we aim to remedy this potential blind spot by augmenting standard APR prompts with program execution traces. We evaluate our approach using the GPT family of models on three popular APR datasets. Our findings suggest that simply incorporating execution traces into the prompt provides a limited performance improvement over trace-free baselines, in only 2 out of 6 tested dataset / model configurations. We further find that the effectiveness of execution traces for APR diminishes as their complexity increases. We explore several strategies for leveraging traces in prompts and demonstrate that LLM-optimized prompts help outperform trace-free prompts more consistently. Additionally, we show trace-based prompting to be superior to finetuning a smaller LLM on a small-scale dataset; and conduct probing studies reinforcing the notion that execution traces can complement the reasoning abilities of the LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of incorporating program execution traces into prompts for Large Language Model (LLM)-based Automatic Program Repair (APR). The authors evaluate various strategies for leveraging traces, including simple inclusion, LLM-optimized traces, and confidence-based prompt selection. They use the GPT family of models on three APR datasets, finding that simply adding execution traces doesn't consistently improve performance.  They analyze the relationship between trace complexity and repair success, discovering that overly long or complex traces can hinder performance. LLM-optimized prompts show more consistent improvements. The paper compares trace-based prompting with fine-tuning a smaller LLM, and conducts probing studies to assess the LLMs' trace understanding abilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic investigation of how execution traces affect LLM-based APR when directly included in prompts *without* significant architectural modifications. This contrasts with prior work that focuses on pre-training LLMs with traces or using traces to guide training. The authors also explore different trace representations and prompt engineering techniques tailored for leveraging execution traces. It's also notable that they investigate this space using two models, OpenAI's GPT 3.5 and GPT-4.

*   **Significance:** The paper provides valuable insights into the challenges and opportunities of using execution traces in LLM-based APR. The finding that simply adding traces isn't always beneficial highlights the need for careful prompt engineering and trace management. The study of trace complexity provides a crucial understanding of how the format and length of traces impact repair success. The probing studies offer insights into the LLMs' trace understanding capabilities. These insights could guide future research towards more effective methods for leveraging dynamic information in program repair.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of different trace integration strategies across multiple datasets and LLMs.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis of trace complexity and its impact on APR performance is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Well-Defined Research Questions:** The research questions are clearly stated and addressed through well-designed experiments.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The findings offer practical guidance for prompt engineering in LLM-based APR.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Trace Analysis:** While the paper considers trace length and variable modifications, a more detailed analysis of the *types* of variable changes and their impact on repair success could be beneficial. For example, differentiating between simple assignments and more complex operations on variables.
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** The datasets are primarily algorithmic and may not fully represent the complexities of real-world software bugs. The paper acknowledges that the realistic dataset involves significant manual effort.
    *   **Reliance on Closed-Source Models:** The use of GPT models makes it difficult to fully understand the models' internal workings and how they process the traces.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Alternative Trace Representations:** While LLM optimization is used, other methods like abstracting the traces to higher levels might be beneficial

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on LLM-based APR by highlighting the importance of careful trace management and prompt engineering. The insights on trace complexity could lead to the development of more effective methods for selecting and representing traces in prompts.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of 6. The paper presents a systematic study with clear findings, but the limitations in dataset complexity, the reliance on closed-source models, and scope of trace analysis somewhat limit its overall impact. The novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary. The results, while solid, don't represent a breakthrough in APR, but rather valuable lessons learned in how to appropriately leverage certain data. Still, the paper should serve as a good reference to the LLM+APR community.

### M2Rec: Multi-scale Mamba for Efficient Sequential Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04445v1)
- **Authors**: Qianru Zhang, Liang Qu, Honggang Wen, Dong Huang, Siu-Ming Yiu, Nguyen Quoc Viet Hung, Hongzhi Yin
- **Abstract**: Sequential recommendation systems aim to predict users' next preferences based on their interaction histories, but existing approaches face critical limitations in efficiency and multi-scale pattern recognition. While Transformer-based methods struggle with quadratic computational complexity, recent Mamba-based models improve efficiency but fail to capture periodic user behaviors, leverage rich semantic information, or effectively fuse multimodal features. To address these challenges, we propose \model, a novel sequential recommendation framework that integrates multi-scale Mamba with Fourier analysis, Large Language Models (LLMs), and adaptive gating. First, we enhance Mamba with Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) to explicitly model periodic patterns in the frequency domain, separating meaningful trends from noise. Second, we incorporate LLM-based text embeddings to enrich sparse interaction data with semantic context from item descriptions. Finally, we introduce a learnable gate mechanism to dynamically balance temporal (Mamba), frequency (FFT), and semantic (LLM) features, ensuring harmonious multimodal fusion. Extensive experiments demonstrate that \model\ achieves state-of-the-art performance, improving Hit Rate@10 by 3.2\% over existing Mamba-based models while maintaining 20\% faster inference than Transformer baselines. Our results highlight the effectiveness of combining frequency analysis, semantic understanding, and adaptive fusion for sequential recommendation. Code and datasets are available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/M2Rec.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "M²Rec: Multi-scale Mamba for Efficient Sequential Recommendation" proposes a novel sequential recommendation framework that combines Mamba (for efficient sequence modeling) with Fast Fourier Transforms (FFT) for explicit modeling of periodic patterns, and Large Language Models (LLMs) for incorporating semantic information from item descriptions. A learnable gating mechanism dynamically balances the contributions of these different modalities. Experiments on several datasets demonstrate improved performance compared to existing Mamba-based and Transformer-based models, with better efficiency than Transformers. The key idea is to address the limitations of existing approaches by explicitly capturing periodic user behaviors, leveraging rich semantic information, and intelligently fusing multimodal features.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Combination of Techniques:** The main strength lies in the innovative integration of Mamba, FFT, LLMs, and a gating mechanism.  While each component has been used individually in recommendation systems, the authors present a compelling architecture that leverages their complementary strengths.  Explicitly modeling periodic patterns with FFT is a valuable addition that addresses a known weakness of standard sequence models.
    *   **Addressing Efficiency Concerns:** The paper directly tackles the efficiency limitations of Transformer-based models by adopting Mamba. The empirical results confirm the reduced inference time compared to Transformer baselines.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper provides thorough experimental evaluation on multiple datasets, comparing against a wide range of competitive baselines (including various Mamba-based models). Ablation studies convincingly demonstrate the importance of each component.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the technical details of the proposed framework. The diagrams and equations are helpful in understanding the architecture.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty (FFT and LLM integration):** While the combination is novel, the individual components themselves are well-established. The core novelty comes from *how* these are fused, rather than inventing new modeling techniques.  The use of FFT in recommendation is not entirely new (FEARec), so the contribution needs to be argued in the context of efficient processing and integration with Mamba.
    *   **Gating Mechanism Simplicity:**  The description of the gating mechanism suggests a relatively simple approach.  A more sophisticated gating architecture could potentially improve the fusion of modalities.
    *   **Limited Exploration of LLM Impact:** While LLMs are used to enrich item representations, the experiments only explore different LLMs briefly. A more in-depth analysis of how different LLMs affect performance and what types of semantic information are most helpful would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** the performance of FFTS is high sensitive to its parameters like cut ratio for frequencies. It might hinder the performance in different datasets.
    *   **Generalizability:** It may not apply to other recommendation scenarios with sparse data and cold start users.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Improved Performance:** The paper demonstrates significant performance improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods. This suggests the potential for M²Rec to be a valuable addition to the toolkit of recommendation system researchers and practitioners.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The improved efficiency makes M²Rec more practical for real-world deployment, especially in scenarios with long sequences and large catalogs.
    *   **Future Research Directions:** The paper opens up several interesting avenues for future research, such as exploring more sophisticated gating mechanisms, investigating different LLMs, and applying M²Rec to other sequential prediction tasks.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a clear and significant advance in sequential recommendation by strategically combining established techniques (Mamba, FFT, LLMs) in a novel and effective way.  The efficiency gains are important, and the experimental results are compelling. However, the individual components lack substantial individual novelty, and some areas (gating mechanism, LLM integration) could be explored in more depth.

### Miipher-2: A Universal Speech Restoration Model for Million-Hour Scale Data Restoration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04457v1)
- **Authors**: Shigeki Karita, Yuma Koizumi, Heiga Zen, Haruko Ishikawa, Robin Scheibler, Michiel Bacchiani
- **Abstract**: Training data cleaning is a new application for generative model-based speech restoration (SR). This paper introduces Miipher-2, an SR model designed for million-hour scale data, for training data cleaning for large-scale generative models like large language models. Key challenges addressed include generalization to unseen languages, operation without explicit conditioning (e.g., text, speaker ID), and computational efficiency. Miipher-2 utilizes a frozen, pre-trained Universal Speech Model (USM), supporting over 300 languages, as a robust, conditioning-free feature extractor. To optimize efficiency and minimize memory, Miipher-2 incorporates parallel adapters for predicting clean USM features from noisy inputs and employs the WaneFit neural vocoder for waveform synthesis. These components were trained on 3,000 hours of multi-lingual, studio-quality recordings with augmented degradations, while USM parameters remained fixed. Experimental results demonstrate Miipher-2's superior or comparable performance to conventional SR models in word-error-rate, speaker similarity, and both objective and subjective sound quality scores across all tested languages. Miipher-2 operates efficiently on consumer-grade accelerators, achieving a real-time factor of 0.0078, enabling the processing of a million-hour speech dataset in approximately three days using only 100 such accelerators.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Miipher-2: A Universal Speech Restoration Model for Million-Hour Scale Data Restoration":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Miipher-2, a speech restoration (SR) model designed for cleaning large-scale speech datasets (million-hour scale) used for training generative models like large language models.  It addresses key challenges such as handling unseen languages, operating without explicit conditioning (e.g., text, speaker ID), and maintaining computational efficiency. Miipher-2 leverages a frozen, pre-trained Universal Speech Model (USM) for robust, conditioning-free feature extraction. Parallel adapters and a modified WaveFit vocoder are incorporated to optimize efficiency and minimize memory consumption. The model is trained on a dataset of studio-quality recordings with added degradations.  Experiments demonstrate Miipher-2's superior or comparable performance to conventional SR models across various languages, with efficient operation on consumer-grade accelerators. The key benefits are faster processing times with lower memory footprints than its predecessors.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper presents incremental novelty building on the initial Miipher work. The key innovations lie in:

    *   **Universal Application:** Shifting from monolingual to multilingual speech restoration using a pre-trained USM model as a fixed feature extractor is a significant step. Leveraging the generalization capabilities of large pre-trained models for this task is novel.
    *   **Efficiency Improvements:**  The use of parallel adapters (PAs) instead of a full Conformer network and modifications to the WaveFit vocoder for reduced memory footprint are important engineering contributions. This is essential for scaling SR to million-hour datasets.
    *   **Conditioning-Free Operation:** Eliminating the need for explicit conditioning features (text, speaker ID) is a valuable simplification, making the model applicable to datasets where such information is unavailable or expensive to obtain.
    *   **Data Distillation:** Showing that training on data cleaned by Miipher-2 performs almost equivalently to studio quality data is interesting for reducing the cost of high-quality data collection.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is substantial, especially given the increasing importance of high-quality data for training large generative models.

    *   **Data Cleaning for Generative Models:** Data quality becomes increasingly important for high-performance models such as LLMs. This research helps address the challenge of obtaining clean speech data, which is inherently more difficult than for text or images.
    *   **Low-Resource Languages:** Miipher-2's ability to handle unseen languages is particularly valuable for training models for low-resource languages, where high-quality data is scarce.
    *   **Scalability:** The emphasis on computational efficiency is crucial for practical application. Enabling the processing of million-hour datasets using relatively accessible hardware (consumer-grade TPUs) democratizes access to large-scale data cleaning.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the architecture and training procedure.
    *   Experimental results are thorough and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach, providing both objective and subjective evaluations.
    *   The computational efficiency analysis is compelling, showing the practical benefits of the proposed optimizations.
    *   The ablations comparing Miipher-2 with its variants is very useful and shows the importance of the architectural improvements.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper relies heavily on pre-trained models (USM), and the novelty is primarily in the fine-tuning and efficiency aspects. While leveraging pre-trained models is a practical approach, it limits the fundamental algorithmic novelty.
    *   The quantitative improvements, while significant, might not always translate to substantial qualitative differences in downstream generative model performance. Showing the impact on, for instance, a downstream TTS model trained on Miipher-2 cleaned data would have reinforced the benefit.
    *   While the authors state that code and checkpoints will not be released, the paper would benefit from open-sourcing the training recipe and detailed architecture specifications.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   Miipher-2 could become a widely used tool for cleaning large-scale speech datasets, enabling the training of better generative models.
    *   The techniques for improving computational efficiency (parallel adapters, modified vocoder) could be adopted in other SR and speech processing applications.
    *   The framework of leveraging pre-trained models for data cleaning could be extended to other modalities and tasks.

*   **Justification of Score:** While building on previous work, the improvements in Miipher-2 allow it to process data at a significantly greater scale. The universal nature is particularly valuable. The improvements in efficiency and the shift to conditioning-free SR are noteworthy and impactful. The reliance on a pre-trained model detracts slightly from the innovation. However, the significance of the engineering contributions and the potential for widespread adoption justify a high score.

### Spectral and Temporal Denoising for Differentially Private Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04468v1)
- **Authors**: Hyeju Shin, Kyudan Jung, Seongwon Yun, Juyoung Yun
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces the FFT-Enhanced Kalman Filter (FFTKF), a differentially private optimization method that addresses the challenge of preserving performance in DP-SGD, where added noise typically degrades model utility. FFTKF integrates frequency-domain noise shaping with Kalman filtering to enhance gradient quality while preserving $(\varepsilon, \delta)$-DP guarantees. It employs a high-frequency shaping mask in the Fourier domain to concentrate differential privacy noise in less informative spectral components, preserving low-frequency gradient signals. A scalar-gain Kalman filter with finite-difference Hessian approximation further refines the denoised gradients. With a per-iteration complexity of $\mathcal{O}(d \log d)$, FFTKF demonstrates improved test accuracy over DP-SGD and DiSK across MNIST, CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and Tiny-ImageNet datasets using CNNs, Wide ResNets, and Vision Transformers. Theoretical analysis confirms that FFTKF maintains equivalent privacy guarantees while achieving a tighter privacy-utility trade-off through reduced noise and controlled bias.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FFT-Enhanced Kalman Filter (FFTKF), a new differentially private optimization method designed to improve performance compared to DP-SGD by reducing the impact of added noise on model utility. FFTKF combines frequency-domain noise shaping via Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) and Kalman filtering to enhance gradient quality. The FFT process concentrates differential privacy noise in high-frequency spectral components (deemed less informative), preserving low-frequency gradient signals. A scalar-gain Kalman filter then refines the gradients. The method is demonstrated to improve test accuracy over DP-SGD and DiSK across several datasets and model architectures while maintaining privacy guarantees. The paper also provides theoretical analysis to support the claimed benefits.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Combination:** The main strength of the paper lies in the combination of two previously explored techniques (frequency domain filtering and Kalman filtering) into a single, coherent framework for differentially private optimization.  While neither component is novel in isolation, their synergistic use is a potentially valuable contribution.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results show consistent improvements over DP-SGD and DiSK (another Kalman filter-based DP optimizer) across diverse datasets (MNIST, CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet) and model architectures (CNNs, Wide ResNets, Vision Transformers). This thorough empirical evaluation supports the practical utility of the method.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The paper presents a theoretical analysis that supports the benefits of FFTKF, including a demonstration that it maintains privacy guarantees while achieving a tighter privacy-utility trade-off through noise reduction and bias control.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The method retains a relatively low per-iteration complexity of O(d log d), making it suitable for large-scale models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The novelty of the individual components is limited. Frequency domain noise shaping and Kalman filtering have been applied to DP before, and the authors do not significantly alter these established methods. The question is whether the specific combination warrants a significant contribution.
    *   **Limited Insight into Parameter Selection:** While the authors demonstrate good performance with particular parameter settings, a more detailed explanation and guidance on how to optimally tune the parameters of FFTKF (e.g., the shaping strength `p`, decay rate `a`, and cutoff index `ko`) for different datasets and model architectures would be valuable.
    *   **Theoretical Limitations:** The theoretical analysis, while supporting, is not ground-breaking.  It reinforces existing insights about the trade-offs between noise reduction, bias, and privacy. The improvement relies on carefully redistributing the privacy noise, which, although conceptually valid, is a nuanced approach where the benefits may vary across diverse real-world situations. The theoretical claims rely on assumptions about the loss function smoothness, which might not always hold.
    *   **Comparison with SOTA:** While comparisons against DP-SGD and DISK are made, a broader comparison with other state-of-the-art DP optimization methods would strengthen the paper's contribution.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper addresses a critical problem in differentially private machine learning: the degradation of model utility due to noise injection. By combining established techniques in a novel way, FFTKF offers a practical approach to improve the privacy-utility trade-off. The potential impact is moderate, offering a further refinement of the process.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a practically useful method for differentially private optimization with demonstrable performance gains. The method is supported by experiments and theory, contributing a valuable, albeit incremental, advance. However, the novelty of the individual components is limited, and the theoretical analysis does not break significantly new ground. Therefore, while offering a useful improvement, FFTKF doesn’t represent a radical shift in the field.

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### TrajEvo: Designing Trajectory Prediction Heuristics via LLM-driven Evolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04480v1)
- **Authors**: Zhikai Zhao, Chuanbo Hua, Federico Berto, Kanghoon Lee, Zihan Ma, Jiachen Li, Jinkyoo Park
- **Abstract**: Trajectory prediction is a crucial task in modeling human behavior, especially in fields as social robotics and autonomous vehicle navigation. Traditional heuristics based on handcrafted rules often lack accuracy, while recently proposed deep learning approaches suffer from computational cost, lack of explainability, and generalization issues that limit their practical adoption. In this paper, we introduce TrajEvo, a framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically design trajectory prediction heuristics. TrajEvo employs an evolutionary algorithm to generate and refine prediction heuristics from past trajectory data. We introduce a Cross-Generation Elite Sampling to promote population diversity and a Statistics Feedback Loop allowing the LLM to analyze alternative predictions. Our evaluations show TrajEvo outperforms previous heuristic methods on the ETH-UCY datasets, and remarkably outperforms both heuristics and deep learning methods when generalizing to the unseen SDD dataset. TrajEvo represents a first step toward automated design of fast, explainable, and generalizable trajectory prediction heuristics. We make our source code publicly available to foster future research at https://github.com/ai4co/trajevo.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TRAJEVO: Designing Trajectory Prediction Heuristics via LLM-driven Evolution":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TRAJEVO, a novel framework that uses Large Language Models (LLMs) within an evolutionary algorithm to automatically design trajectory prediction heuristics.  Traditional heuristic methods often lack accuracy and generalization capabilities, while deep learning methods are computationally expensive, not explainable, and have generalization issues. TRAJEVO aims to bridge this gap by leveraging LLMs to generate, evaluate, and refine trajectory prediction heuristics directly from trajectory data. Key components include a Cross-Generation Elite Sampling strategy to maintain population diversity and a Statistics Feedback Loop that enables the LLM to analyze heuristic performance.  The authors demonstrate that TRAJEVO outperforms existing heuristic methods on the ETH-UCY dataset and achieves remarkable generalization to the unseen SDD dataset, even surpassing deep learning methods in this out-of-distribution setting, while retaining computational efficiency and interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs within an evolutionary algorithm to design heuristics for trajectory prediction is genuinely novel. While LLMs have been used for code generation and other algorithmic design tasks, their application to *specifically* automate the development of trajectory prediction heuristics is a unique contribution. The design of the Cross-Generation Elite Sampling and the Statistics Feedback Loop are also novel and tailored for this specific application.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in trajectory prediction: the trade-off between accuracy, computational cost, explainability, and generalization. TRAJEVO's ability to generate heuristics that are both accurate and computationally efficient, while also being interpretable, has the potential to make trajectory prediction more accessible for real-world robotic applications. The out-of-distribution generalization results are especially impactful, as this is a common weakness of deep learning-based methods. The ability to generate explainable code is valuable, as it provides insight on effective strategies for generating trajectory predictions in challenging environments.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** A truly unique and creative approach to the problem.
*   **Strong Results:** Demonstrates significant improvements in generalization and competitive performance on standard benchmarks.
*   **Interpretability:** Generates code that provides insight into the decision-making process.
*   **Computational Efficiency:** Achieves a significant speedup compared to deep learning approaches.
*   **Well-Written and Clear:** The paper is easy to understand and presents the results clearly.
*   **Addresses a relevant limitation:** The work reduces the reliance on hand-crafted algorithms by automating the algorithmic design of trajectory prediction strategies, removing the need to depend on human expertise on algorithmic structure.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **In-Distribution Performance:** While TRAJEVO achieves state-of-the-art results for heuristics, it does not consistently outperform the best deep learning methods *in-distribution* on the ETH-UCY datasets. This suggests that there may still be room for improvement in the accuracy of the generated heuristics.
*   **Limited Input Data Complexity:** The current implementation relies primarily on positional history and doesn't incorporate other sensor data or contextual information.
*   **Downstream task performance:** The performance is measured on standard trajectory prediction metrics which might not align directly with robotic tasks, thus a closed loop evaluation with robotic tasks would make the results more impactful.
*   **Computational requirements for evolution:** While the inference of evolved heuristics is efficient, the method requires querying an LLM to evolve the heuristics, which can be costly.
*   **Evaluation on few datasets:** It might be interesting to study how different datasets influence the heuristic's structure and properties.

**Potential Influence:**

TRAJEVO has the potential to influence the field of trajectory prediction by offering a new paradigm for algorithm design. Its ability to generate explainable and computationally efficient heuristics can facilitate the adoption of trajectory prediction in real-world applications, especially those with resource constraints. It could also spur further research into combining LLMs and evolutionary algorithms for other robotics tasks. In particular, TRAJEVO encourages the design of new automated algorithmic design strategies that can improve the state of the art on specific domains.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the paper's novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of 8 is warranted.  The approach is highly novel, the results on generalization are strong and the potential influence is significant. However, the limitations regarding in-distribution performance, input data complexity, limited evaluation metrics, and computational requirements for the evolutionary procedure hold it back from receiving a higher score. The paper makes a significant contribution, but it is not a game-changer that will fundamentally transform the field overnight.

**Score: 8**

- **Score**: 8/10

### CAD-Llama: Leveraging Large Language Models for Computer-Aided Design Parametric 3D Model Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04481v1)
- **Authors**: Jiahao Li, Weijian Ma, Xueyang Li, Yunzhong Lou, Guichun Zhou, Xiangdong Zhou
- **Abstract**: Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved significant success, prompting increased interest in expanding their generative capabilities beyond general text into domain-specific areas. This study investigates the generation of parametric sequences for computer-aided design (CAD) models using LLMs. This endeavor represents an initial step towards creating parametric 3D shapes with LLMs, as CAD model parameters directly correlate with shapes in three-dimensional space. Despite the formidable generative capacities of LLMs, this task remains challenging, as these models neither encounter parametric sequences during their pretraining phase nor possess direct awareness of 3D structures. To address this, we present CAD-Llama, a framework designed to enhance pretrained LLMs for generating parametric 3D CAD models. Specifically, we develop a hierarchical annotation pipeline and a code-like format to translate parametric 3D CAD command sequences into Structured Parametric CAD Code (SPCC), incorporating hierarchical semantic descriptions. Furthermore, we propose an adaptive pretraining approach utilizing SPCC, followed by an instruction tuning process aligned with CAD-specific guidelines. This methodology aims to equip LLMs with the spatial knowledge inherent in parametric sequences. Experimental results demonstrate that our framework significantly outperforms prior autoregressive methods and existing LLM baselines.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces CAD-Llama, a novel framework designed to enhance large language models (LLMs) for generating parametric 3D CAD models from text prompts. The key innovation is the introduction of Structured Parametric CAD Code (SPCC), a code-like format and hierarchical annotation pipeline that translates CAD command sequences into structured semantic descriptions. The authors also propose an adaptive pretraining approach utilizing SPCC, followed by instruction tuning tailored to CAD-specific guidelines. The experimental results demonstrate that CAD-Llama outperforms prior autoregressive methods and existing LLM baselines in generating more complex and accurate CAD models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the SPCC format and the associated hierarchical annotation pipeline. While LLMs have been explored in CAD generation, the structured representation of CAD commands with rich semantic descriptions, enabling better learning, is a significant contribution. The adaptive pretraining and instruction tuning are also novel applications within this specific context.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in CAD generative modeling: bridging the gap between natural language and parametric CAD sequences. By enabling LLMs to generate complex CAD models from text prompts, CAD-Llama has the potential to significantly impact the field of CAD design, making it more accessible and efficient.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined framework with clear contributions.
    *   Thorough experimental validation demonstrating superior performance.
    *   Hierarchical annotation pipeline addresses the challenge of capturing complex CAD semantics.
    *   SPCC format leverages LLM's code generation capabilities effectively.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on GPT-4 for annotation introduces a dependency on a closed-source model. While understandable for research exploration, this potentially impacts reproducibility and scalability, especially considering future API changes or availability.
    *   While the paper compares against existing methods, a more detailed ablation study could further elucidate the contribution of each component (e.g., SPCC vs. adaptive pretraining vs. instruction tuning). The additional ablation in the supplementary materials does add to this point, however.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of limitations, such as the complexity of CAD models it can handle and potential issues with generalization to unseen CAD datasets. Section E.6 in the supplementary material does begin to address this, however.
    *   While the Fusion 360 cross-dataset experiments are promising, more details on data cleaning, balancing, and the range of designs would strengthen this section.

Despite these minor weaknesses, the paper presents a solid and valuable contribution to the field of CAD generative modeling. The SPCC format and hierarchical annotation pipeline are well-designed, and the experimental results clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of CAD-Llama in generating complex and accurate CAD models from text prompts. The work has the potential to influence future research in this area and significantly impact the practice of CAD design.

### Efficient Flow Matching using Latent Variables
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04486v1)
- **Authors**: Anirban Samaddar, Yixuan Sun, Viktor Nilsson, Sandeep Madireddy
- **Abstract**: Flow matching models have shown great potential in image generation tasks among probabilistic generative models. Building upon the ideas of continuous normalizing flows, flow matching models generalize the transport path of the diffusion models from a simple prior distribution to the data. Most flow matching models in the literature do not explicitly model the underlying structure/manifold in the target data when learning the flow from a simple source distribution like the standard Gaussian. This leads to inefficient learning, especially for many high-dimensional real-world datasets, which often reside in a low-dimensional manifold. Existing strategies of incorporating manifolds, including data with underlying multi-modal distribution, often require expensive training and hence frequently lead to suboptimal performance. To this end, we present \texttt{Latent-CFM}, which provides simplified training/inference strategies to incorporate multi-modal data structures using pretrained deep latent variable models. Through experiments on multi-modal synthetic data and widely used image benchmark datasets, we show that \texttt{Latent-CFM} exhibits improved generation quality with significantly less training ($\sim 50\%$ less in some cases) and computation than state-of-the-art flow matching models. Using a 2d Darcy flow dataset, we demonstrate that our approach generates more physically accurate samples than competitive approaches. In addition, through latent space analysis, we demonstrate that our approach can be used for conditional image generation conditioned on latent features.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Latent-CFM, a new flow-matching approach that leverages pretrained deep latent variable models (LVMs) to improve image generation quality and training efficiency.  Unlike standard flow matching models that directly transport a simple prior distribution (e.g., Gaussian) to the data, Latent-CFM first maps the data into a lower-dimensional latent space using a pretrained LVM.  The flow matching process then occurs in this latent space, allowing the model to exploit the underlying structure and manifold of the data. The authors demonstrate improved generation quality with less training and computation compared to state-of-the-art flow-matching models on synthetic, image benchmark, and even physical datasets like Darcy flow. Further, it shows that this method can also be used for conditional image generation based on latent features.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Improved Efficiency and Quality:** The primary strength lies in demonstrating a significant improvement in training efficiency (up to 50% reduction in training steps) while maintaining or improving generation quality compared to standard flow-matching techniques. This is a practical and valuable contribution.
*   **Handling Multimodality:** The paper highlights the ability of Latent-CFM to capture multi-modal data distributions more effectively than other conditional flow matching (CFM) methods like OT-CFM and I-CFM. The visual examples on the synthetic triangle dataset are compelling.
*   **Scientific Applications:**  The application to the 2D Darcy flow dataset is noteworthy. Showing that Latent-CFM can generate more physically accurate samples (as measured by PDE residuals) suggests the potential for this approach in scientific machine learning where incorporating domain knowledge/constraints is crucial.
*   **Latent Space Analysis:** The analysis of the learned latent space demonstrates a potential for interpretable and controllable generation. The ability to generate images conditioned on specific latent features opens up possibilities for conditional image generation and data exploration.
*   **Leveraging Existing Models:**  The approach relies on pretrained LVMs, making it relatively easy to adapt existing models and infrastructure. This reduces the barrier to adoption and allows for leveraging advances in LVM research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on LVM Quality:** The performance of Latent-CFM is tied to the quality of the pretrained LVM. While the authors use popular VAEs, the choice of LVM architecture and training procedure could significantly impact the results. A more thorough discussion on the sensitivity of Latent-CFM to the LVM characteristics would strengthen the paper.
*   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While comparisons to other flow matching methods are included, a more detailed ablation study would be beneficial. For example, exploring the impact of different latent dimensions or regularization strategies (e.g. varying the KL-divergence weight) could provide deeper insights.
*   **Complexity of Inference:** Although training is demonstrated to be more efficient, it's important to critically examine any increase in complexity in the *inference* stage as well.  The need to map to latent space prior to generation adds steps, and this might impact real-time applications. A discussion of the computational overhead during inference would be helpful.
*   **Clarity of Justification for Upper Bound:** In Section A, the justification is only based on L2 norm of the vector field. This approach is only good for single vector field estimation rather than the vector field over a distribution.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The core idea of integrating pretrained latent variable models into the flow matching framework is novel.  Previous work on incorporating data structure in flow-based models has either relied on specialized flows, modifying the source distribution, or expensive training strategies. Latent-CFM offers a more simplified and stable training and inference procedure.  The demonstrated improvements in efficiency and generation quality, especially on multimodal data and physical datasets, highlight the significance of this approach.

**Potential Influence:**

Latent-CFM has the potential to influence the direction of research in flow-based generative models. The paper provides a practical and effective way to exploit the underlying structure of complex data, making flow matching more applicable to real-world problems. The demonstrated improvements in training efficiency are also highly valuable, as they address a key limitation of many generative models. Moreover, the potential of the approach in solving real-world physical systems and scientific simulation offers significant potential.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

Latent-CFM represents a solid contribution to the field. The core idea is innovative and offers tangible benefits in terms of training efficiency and generation quality. The application to physical datasets strengthens the paper's significance. However, the reliance on pretrained LVMs and limited ablation studies prevent a higher score. Addressing these limitations and providing a more in-depth analysis of the trade-offs involved in Latent-CFM could further enhance the paper's impact. While there are potential issues with the justification for upper-bounding LCFM by LLatent-CFM, the proposed method still allows access to approximate posterior likelihoods qâ (.|x) which can then be used for classifier guided generation of images. The clarity of the proposed method implementation and experimental setup are clear in most cases, and the significance in the field of scientific simulation cannot be ignored.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Defining and Quantifying Creative Behavior in Popular Image Generators
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04497v1)
- **Authors**: Aditi Ramaswamy
- **Abstract**: Creativity of generative AI models has been a subject of scientific debate in the last years, without a conclusive answer. In this paper, we study creativity from a practical perspective and introduce quantitative measures that help the user to choose a suitable AI model for a given task. We evaluated our measures on a number of popular image-to-image generation models, and the results of this suggest that our measures conform to human intuition.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the subjective concept of creativity in generative AI models, particularly focusing on image-to-image (img2img) models. It proposes a method for quantifying task-oriented creativity through mathematical measures that capture the satisfaction of prompt requirements, cohesion between output artifacts, and diversity of the outputs. The method involves an iterative "chain construction" inspired by the telephone game, where outputs are fed back as inputs for subsequent generations.  The authors test their measures on popular img2img models (Stable Diffusion, Kandinsky, FLUX) using various input temperatures and analyze the resulting chains using object detection and semantic similarity metrics.  The results indicate that image input temperature affects the measures and that textual inputs (captions) are crucial for producing creative and faithful outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a potentially novel approach to quantifying creativity in img2img models. While the concept of creativity in AI is a well-trodden area, the authors tackle it from a pragmatic, task-oriented perspective, and the chain construction methodology appears to be a unique contribution.  The focus on img2img models, as opposed to primarily large language models and text-to-image models as the authors point out, is also a valuable focus.
* **Significance:** The significance lies in the development of metrics that could aid users in selecting suitable models for specific tasks. Furthermore, the quantitative data and analysis can inform developers about model strengths and weaknesses, guiding future training and architectural decisions. The identified importance of both image input temperature and textual descriptions offers actionable insights.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clearly Defined Metrics:**  The paper provides formal definitions for satisfaction, cohesion, and diversity, making the methodology reproducible and amenable to further research.
    * **Iterative Approach:** The chain construction offers a more realistic simulation of how users interact with generative models, allowing for the capture of evolving creativity over multiple iterations.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The experiments on multiple img2img models with varying parameters provide empirical evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed measures.
    * **Identification of Key Factors:** The paper highlights the interplay between image input temperature and textual captions in influencing creative outputs.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Object Detection and Semantic Similarity:** The method relies heavily on the accuracy of object detectors (GROUNDINGDINO, DETR) and semantic similarity metrics (SPACY). The authors acknowledge this limitation, noting that imperfections in these tools can impact the results.  A more robust approach might involve incorporating human evaluation to validate the automatically generated scores.
    * **Simplicity of Seed Images:**  Using only simple seed images (apple pie, donuts, pizza) might limit the generalizability of the findings.  Exploring more complex and diverse datasets would strengthen the conclusions. The argument that these are the easiest to measure requirement satisfaction, cohesion, and diversity is a bit weak because the impact of errors from the detectors might dominate.
    * **Low Creativity Scores:** The consistently low creativity scores across all models suggest that the proposed measures might be overly restrictive or that the models tested are inherently limited in their creative abilities. More analysis is needed to understand the source of these low scores.
    * **Potential Overlap in Metrics:** The metrics of cohesion and diversity, while distinct in theory, may exhibit some overlap in practice. This could potentially skew the overall creativity ranking.
* **Impact:** The paper's impact depends on its adoption by the research community and industry. If the proposed measures prove to be useful for model evaluation and selection, it could contribute to the development of more creative and controllable generative AI systems.
* **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and provides sufficient details for replicating the experiments. The definitions of the metrics are clear and concise.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and novel approach to a challenging problem: quantifying creativity in generative AI. While the reliance on existing object detection tools and the use of simple seed images represent limitations, the overall methodology, clear definitions, and empirical validation make it a significant contribution. The identification of image input temperature and textual inputs as key factors is particularly noteworthy. A higher score could have been warranted had the experiments included a human evaluation component.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Pangu Ultra MoE: How to Train Your Big MoE on Ascend NPUs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04519v1)
- **Authors**: Yehui Tang, Yichun Yin, Yaoyuan Wang, Hang Zhou, Yu Pan, Wei Guo, Ziyang Zhang, Miao Rang, Fangcheng Liu, Naifu Zhang, Binghan Li, Yonghan Dong, Xiaojun Meng, Yasheng Wang, Dong Li, Yin Li, Dandan Tu, Can Chen, Youliang Yan, Fisher Yu, Ruiming Tang, Yunhe Wang, Botian Huang, Bo Wang, Boxiao Liu, Changzheng Zhang, Da Kuang, Fei Liu, Gang Huang, Jiansheng Wei, Jiarui Qin, Jie Ran, Jinpeng Li, Jun Zhao, Liang Dai, Lin Li, Liqun Deng, Peifeng Qin, Pengyuan Zeng, Qiang Gu, Shaohua Tang, Shengjun Cheng, Tao Gao, Tao Yu, Tianshu Li, Tianyu Bi, Wei He, Weikai Mao, Wenyong Huang, Wulong Liu, Xiabing Li, Xianzhi Yu, Xueyu Wu, Xu He, Yangkai Du, Yan Xu, Ye Tian, Yimeng Wu, Yongbing Huang, Yong Tian, Yong Zhu, Yue Li, Yufei Wang, Yuhang Gai, Yujun Li, Yu Luo, Yunsheng Ni, Yusen Sun, Zelin Chen, Zhe Liu, Zhicheng Liu, Zhipeng Tu, Zilin Ding, Zongyuan Zhan
- **Abstract**: Sparse large language models (LLMs) with Mixture of Experts (MoE) and close to a trillion parameters are dominating the realm of most capable language models. However, the massive model scale poses significant challenges for the underlying software and hardware systems. In this paper, we aim to uncover a recipe to harness such scale on Ascend NPUs. The key goals are better usage of the computing resources under the dynamic sparse model structures and materializing the expected performance gain on the actual hardware. To select model configurations suitable for Ascend NPUs without repeatedly running the expensive experiments, we leverage simulation to compare the trade-off of various model hyperparameters. This study led to Pangu Ultra MoE, a sparse LLM with 718 billion parameters, and we conducted experiments on the model to verify the simulation results. On the system side, we dig into Expert Parallelism to optimize the communication between NPU devices to reduce the synchronization overhead. We also optimize the memory efficiency within the devices to further reduce the parameter and activation management overhead. In the end, we achieve an MFU of 30.0% when training Pangu Ultra MoE, with performance comparable to that of DeepSeek R1, on 6K Ascend NPUs, and demonstrate that the Ascend system is capable of harnessing all the training stages of the state-of-the-art language models. Extensive experiments indicate that our recipe can lead to efficient training of large-scale sparse language models with MoE. We also study the behaviors of such models for future reference.
- **Summary**: This technical report presents Pangu Ultra MoE, a 718 billion parameter sparse language model designed for efficient training on Ascend NPUs. The authors focus on optimizing both the model architecture and the system to achieve high hardware utilization. They detail a simulation-based approach to model architecture exploration, followed by system-level optimizations such as hierarchical expert parallelism, adaptive pipe overlap, fine-grained recomputation, and dynamic load balancing. They report a Model Flops Utilization (MFU) of 30.0% on 6K Ascend NPUs, comparable to DeepSeek R1, and analyze expert specialization and co-activation patterns within the MoE architecture. Finally, the report validates the model's performance across several benchmarks, including those for general language comprehension, reasoning, and industry-specific tasks.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper demonstrates a significant engineering effort in adapting MoE models to the Ascend NPU platform. The methodology of combining simulation-driven architecture exploration with targeted system-level optimizations is valuable. The focus on achieving high MFU is essential for practical application, and the reported result of 30% is certainly commendable.

However, the paper has several weaknesses. The description of the simulation framework is somewhat vague, making it difficult to assess its sophistication and accuracy.  While the system optimizations are discussed, the depth of technical detail is uneven, and the specific implementations of certain optimizations (e.g., the kernel fusion) are not explained sufficiently. Furthermore, the evaluation results, although comprehensive, lack a direct comparison with a similarly sized dense model on the same hardware. This makes it harder to fully gauge the claimed efficiency gains of the sparse architecture, instead we are given a comparison to DeepSeek R1, which may or may not be running on the same hardware.

Novelty is somewhat limited. While the combination of various optimization techniques is beneficial, many of the individual techniques (expert parallelism, pipeline parallelism, recomputation) are known in the literature. The main contributions are the specific adaptations and configurations for Ascend NPUs, and the demonstration of their effectiveness at scale. The inclusion of expert analysis (domain specialization and co-activation) adds some value but is not the primary focus. The report also does not include a thorough discussion of the limitations of the work, particularly concerning the specific Ascend NPU architecture and the generalizability to other hardware platforms. The fact that the team is from Huawei inherently raises some concerns about independent replication of the results, even though the results are interesting and promising, given the current geopolitical climate. The lack of open-sourcing the model also limits the community's ability to verify and build upon the work.

Significance: The significance lies in demonstrating that Ascend NPUs can effectively train large-scale MoE models, potentially fostering wider adoption of this hardware platform. The insights into model configuration and system optimization are relevant to others working with sparse models, particularly on similar architectures. The validation of Pangu Ultra MoE across various benchmarks highlights its practical capabilities and could stimulate further research in efficient MoE training techniques.

Score: 6/10

Rationale:

The paper presents a substantial engineering effort with demonstrable results. The combination of simulation and system-level optimizations provides a valuable, albeit specific, blueprint for training large MoE models on Ascend NPUs. However, limited novelty in individual techniques and the proprietary nature of the work, along with missing comparisons and depth of detail, prevent a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Comparative Analysis of Carbon Footprint in Manual vs. LLM-Assisted Code Development
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04521v1)
- **Authors**: Kuen Sum Cheung, Mayuri Kaul, Gunel Jahangirova, Mohammad Reza Mousavi, Eric Zie
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLM) have significantly transformed various domains, including software development. These models assist programmers in generating code, potentially increasing productivity and efficiency. However, the environmental impact of utilising these AI models is substantial, given their high energy consumption during both training and inference stages. This research aims to compare the energy consumption of manual software development versus an LLM-assisted approach, using Codeforces as a simulation platform for software development. The goal is to quantify the environmental impact and propose strategies for minimising the carbon footprint of using LLM in software development. Our results show that the LLM-assisted code generation leads on average to 32.72 higher carbon footprint than the manual one. Moreover, there is a significant correlation between task complexity and the difference in the carbon footprint of the two approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the carbon footprint of using Large Language Models (LLMs) to assist in software development, comparing it to traditional manual coding. It utilizes Codeforces as a platform to simulate and analyze programming tasks, measuring energy consumption during coding, testing, and debugging in both scenarios. The study finds that LLM-assisted code generation leads to a significantly higher carbon footprint than manual coding, and this difference correlates positively with task complexity. The research proposes strategies for minimizing the environmental impact of LLMs in software development, such as task decomposition and complexity-aware process adjustment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a relevant and increasingly important question: the environmental impact of AI-assisted software development. While the general concern about LLM energy consumption is known, a direct comparative analysis focusing on *software development tasks* is relatively novel. Earlier works often focused on training costs, or evaluating the *code* sustainability, rather than the entire development *process*. This specific comparison of LLM-assisted versus manual coding carbon footprint, using an empirical approach with Codeforces data, is what constitutes the paper's main novelty.

*   **Significance:** The findings have potentially significant implications. By quantifying the higher carbon footprint of LLM-assisted coding, the paper raises awareness about the trade-offs between productivity gains and environmental costs. This is crucial for informed decision-making in software engineering practices, particularly as LLMs become more integrated into the development workflow. The recommended best practices, while preliminary, provide a starting point for more sustainable LLM usage.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Empirical Approach:** The use of Codeforces provides a realistic (albeit simulated) environment for comparing the two development approaches. The large dataset of user submissions adds statistical robustness.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The methodology for estimating energy consumption, broken down into coding, debugging, testing, and LLM-related components, is well-defined and transparent.
    *   **Relevant Research Questions:** The research questions directly address the central issue of the study and drive the analysis effectively.
    *   **Quantified Results:** The paper provides concrete quantitative results, like the ~33x larger carbon footprint of LLM-assisted coding, making the findings impactful and memorable.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:**  The suggestion to decompose complex tasks into smaller ones and adjust development process as a function of estimated LLM complexity offers tangible guidance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses solely on a subset of software development activities (implementation, testing, debugging). It does not cover design, requirements gathering, or deployment, which are also energy-intensive stages. The focus is also limited to a single LLM, GPT-4, and a single programming language, Python.
    *   **Assumptions and Estimations:** Several estimations and assumptions are made, particularly concerning energy consumption parameters and debugging time, which could affect the accuracy of the absolute carbon footprint values. While relative comparison is still valuable, these assumptions warrant caution when generalizing the specific numbers.  The use of publicly available hardware specifications rather than direct power meter measurements introduces a level of uncertainty.  The estimation method is sound, however, it would be made much stronger with physical measurement.
    *   **Simplified Simulation:** Using Codeforces as a real development enviornment has some caveats. The code is written to solve very specific problems, is run once, and is not maintained, which is quite different from real software engineering.
    *   **Competitive Programming Bias:** The use of Codeforces tasks might bias the results towards specific types of problems (algorithmic, problem-solving), which could not fully represent the broad spectrum of software development challenges.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on sustainable software engineering and LLM usage. It could encourage further investigation into the environmental costs of AI-assisted development and lead to the development of more energy-efficient LLMs and development tools. It provides a baseline and methodology for others to build upon. It raises critical questions regarding sustainability, which could inform the development of tools to measure and analyse carbon costs in development.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable and novel contribution by providing an empirical comparison of the carbon footprints of manual vs. LLM-assisted code development. The methodology is sound, and the results are significant. However, the limitations regarding scope, assumptions, and generalizability prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking study. The dependence on Codeforces as a proxy for all software development is significant. Therefore, the score reflects a strong but not exceptional contribution.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Text2CT: Towards 3D CT Volume Generation from Free-text Descriptions Using Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04522v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Guo, Can Zhao, Dong Yang, Yufan He, Vishwesh Nath, Ziyue Xu, Pedro R. A. S. Bassi, Zongwei Zhou, Benjamin D. Simon, Stephanie Anne Harmon, Baris Turkbey, Daguang Xu
- **Abstract**: Generating 3D CT volumes from descriptive free-text inputs presents a transformative opportunity in diagnostics and research. In this paper, we introduce Text2CT, a novel approach for synthesizing 3D CT volumes from textual descriptions using the diffusion model. Unlike previous methods that rely on fixed-format text input, Text2CT employs a novel prompt formulation that enables generation from diverse, free-text descriptions. The proposed framework encodes medical text into latent representations and decodes them into high-resolution 3D CT scans, effectively bridging the gap between semantic text inputs and detailed volumetric representations in a unified 3D framework. Our method demonstrates superior performance in preserving anatomical fidelity and capturing intricate structures as described in the input text. Extensive evaluations show that our approach achieves state-of-the-art results, offering promising potential applications in diagnostics, and data augmentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Text2CT" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Text2CT, a novel framework for generating 3D CT volumes from free-text descriptions using a diffusion model. It addresses limitations of existing methods by proposing a unified 3D architecture (a 3D compression network and a text-conditional Latent Diffusion Model) that handles high memory consumption and constraints imposed by fixed-format text inputs. The method uses a new prompt formulation with Large Language Models (LLMs) to translate radiology reports into diverse textual inputs, allowing the model to capture diverse clinical narratives. The paper demonstrates that Text2CT outperforms baselines in image quality, text alignment, and data augmentation tasks. It also presents human expert assessments confirming the clinical applicability of the generated volumes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelties lie in:
    *   A fully 3D framework to generate high-resolution CT volumes.
    *   Employing an LLM to enable free-text input, unlike previous methods that require specific formats.
    *   The combination of a 3D Compression Network with a text-conditional diffusion model for efficient memory management.

While diffusion models for image generation and text-to-image synthesis are established, their application to high-resolution 3D *medical* volumes with free-text input demonstrates a significant advancement. The framework addresses the substantial computational demands and the need for anatomical accuracy that are crucial in the medical domain. It's a non-trivial extension and integration of existing techniques.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of Text2CT is significant:
    *   **Data Augmentation:** The method can generate synthetic medical data to augment limited datasets, addressing the data scarcity problem that is a hurdle for developing DL models in the medical field.
    *   **Clinical Applications:**  By enabling tailored visualizations of patient-specific conditions from descriptive text, Text2CT holds the potential to revolutionize diagnostics and personalized treatment planning.
    *   **Accessibility:** Enables healthcare professionals to input more personalized and context-specific information, leading to more accurate and clinically relevant 3D medical image generation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation against state-of-the-art methods.
    *   Incorporation of human expert assessments, increasing confidence in the clinical relevance.
    *   Ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of each component.
    * Addresses important challenges such as memory limitation and fixed input format.
    * The paper is well-written and the methodology is clearly explained.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The model performance depends on the quality and detail of the input text. (This is, to some extent, inherent in the task).
    *   Despite optimization efforts, the high computational demands associated with generating 3D volumes may restrict its use in resource-constrained settings.
    *   The model relies on pre-trained models such as MAISI VAE, which can introduce bias.

*   **Overall:**

The paper presents a strong contribution to medical imaging. The proposed Text2CT model addresses significant challenges in generating high-quality, anatomically accurate 3D CT volumes from flexible clinical text prompts. The approach is well-designed, validated with robust experiments, and shows potential to positively impact medical imaging applications. While there are limitations, the novelty and significance of the work justify a high score.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper showcases a significant advancement in generating realistic 3D CT volumes from free-text inputs, bridging the gap between semantic text and detailed volumetric representations. The system addresses significant technical challenges (memory usage, fixed-format inputs) and is rigorously evaluated. The LLM-based prompt formulation provides an innovative approach to handle diverse clinical narratives. While improvements can be made, the overall contribution is substantial and presents a clear step forward in medical imaging and AI.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Fight Fire with Fire: Defending Against Malicious RL Fine-Tuning via Reward Neutralization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04578v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjun Cao
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning transforms large language models while creating a vulnerability we experimentally verify: Our experiment shows that malicious RL fine-tuning dismantles safety guardrails with remarkable efficiency, requiring only 50 steps and minimal adversarial prompts, with harmful escalating from 0-2 to 7-9. This attack vector particularly threatens open-source models with parameter-level access.
