Existing defenses targeting supervised fine-tuning prove ineffective against RL's dynamic feedback mechanisms. We introduce Reward Neutralization, the first defense framework specifically designed against RL fine-tuning attacks, establishing concise rejection patterns that render malicious reward signals ineffective. Our approach trains models to produce minimal-information rejections that attackers cannot exploit, systematically neutralizing attempts to optimize toward harmful outputs. Experiments validate that our approach maintains low harmful scores (no greater than 2) after 200 attack steps, while standard models rapidly deteriorate. This work provides the first constructive proof that robust defense against increasingly accessible RL attacks is achievable, addressing a critical security gap for open-weight models.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the document. Here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper "Fight Fire with Fire: Defending Against Malicious RL Fine-Tuning via Reward Neutralization" addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to malicious reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning attacks. The authors demonstrate that such attacks can efficiently dismantle safety guardrails, requiring only a few steps to induce harmful behavior. They propose a defense mechanism called "Reward Neutralization," which involves training models to produce minimal-information rejections to render malicious reward signals ineffective. The authors show that their approach maintains low harmful scores after many attack steps across different model architectures and harm domains, suggesting a robust defense against RL-based attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel defense framework, Reward Neutralization, specifically designed for countering RL fine-tuning attacks, which is a relatively new attack vector compared to traditional supervised fine-tuning attacks. Existing defenses are shown to be ineffective against these RL-based attacks. The core idea of training models to give concise, minimal-information rejections that are difficult for attackers to reward is innovative and well-motivated.
*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it addresses a crucial security gap in open-weight LLMs. With increasing access to model parameters, the threat of malicious RL fine-tuning becomes more pronounced. The paper provides the first constructive proof that robust defense against such attacks is achievable. This is important for the responsible development and deployment of LLMs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear problem statement:** The paper clearly articulates the threat posed by malicious RL fine-tuning and highlights the inadequacy of existing defenses.
*   **Well-motivated approach:** The rationale behind Reward Neutralization is sound and logically derived from the properties of RL fine-tuning and how it differs from supervised fine-tuning.
*   **Empirical validation:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of Reward Neutralization in mitigating RL attacks across diverse model architectures and harm domains.
*   **Theoretical Analysis**: Providing a more detailed analysis of the RL attack in the form of equation 2 is a nice addition to support the methodology.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Includes 3 widely-used open source language models: LLaMA3-8B, Qwen2.5-7B, and Ministral-8B.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Evaluation**: The validation of Reward Neutralization focuses on a limited number of harm categories. The paper would be more robust if it demonstrated its effectiveness on a more expansive set of potentially harmful prompts.
*   **Generalizability**: The paper does not deeply delve into how Reward Neutralization influences LLMs' capabilities or whether it compromises any other useful characteristics of LLMs. It is essential to examine if the proposed defence method changes the model's downstream task performances.
*   **Computational Expense**: There is no discussion on the costs involved in Reward Neutralization in terms of the extra model training time.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper.

*   The paper addresses a very important and timely problem. The threat of malicious RL fine-tuning attacks on LLMs is a realistic and growing concern.
*   The Reward Neutralization approach is novel and well-motivated. The idea of using minimal-information rejections to disrupt reward signals is innovative and could be a valuable contribution to the field.
*   The empirical results provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of Reward Neutralization.

However, there are a few weaknesses. The evaluation could be more comprehensive, and the analysis of potential side effects of Reward Neutralization is lacking. There is no discussion on cost in terms of model training time. Nevertheless, this paper provides a strong foundation for future research on defending against RL attacks on LLMs and presents a worthwhile advancement in the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SlideItRight: Using AI to Find Relevant Slides and Provide Feedback for Open-Ended Questions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04584v1)
- **Authors**: Chloe Qianhui Zhao, Jie Cao, Eason Chen, Kenneth R. Koedinger, Jionghao Lin
- **Abstract**: Feedback is important in supporting student learning. While various automated feedback systems have been implemented to make the feedback scalable, many existing solutions only focus on generating text-based feedback. As is indicated in the multimedia learning principle, learning with more modalities could help utilize more separate channels, reduce the cognitive load and facilitate students' learning. Hence, it is important to explore the potential of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in feedback generation from and to different modalities. Our study leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) for textual feedback with the supplementary guidance from other modality - relevant lecture slide retrieved from the slides hub. Through an online crowdsourcing study (N=91), this study investigates learning gains and student perceptions using a 2x2 design (i.e., human feedback vs. AI feedback and with vs. without relevant slide), evaluating the clarity, engagement, perceived effectiveness, and reliability) of AI-facilitated multimodal feedback. We observed significant pre-to-post learning gains across all conditions. However, the differences in these gains were not statistically significant between conditions. The post-survey revealed that students found the slide feedback helpful in their learning process, though they reported difficulty in understanding it. Regarding the AI-generated open-ended feedback, students considered it personalized and relevant to their responses, but they expressed lower trust in the AI feedback compared to human-generated feedback.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper, "SlideItRight: Using AI to Find Relevant Slides and Provide Feedback for Open-Ended Questions," explores the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate textual feedback for students, supplemented by relevant lecture slides retrieved from a slide hub. The study investigates learning gains and student perceptions through a 2x2 online crowdsourcing study (human vs. AI feedback, with vs. without relevant slides).  The authors measured learning gains using pre- and post-tests and assessed student perceptions of clarity, engagement, perceived effectiveness, and reliability via surveys. While learning gains were observed in all conditions, no statistically significant differences were found between conditions. Student surveys revealed that participants found the slide feedback helpful, despite comprehension challenges, and viewed AI-generated feedback as personalized and relevant, but with lower trust compared to human feedback.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The integration of LLMs with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) specifically for multimodal (textual feedback + relevant slide) feedback is a valuable contribution. RAG and LLMs are active areas, but the focused application on open-ended question feedback with slide integration provides a degree of novelty. Existing work on LLM-based feedback often focuses on textual output alone or utilizes simplified multimodal input. The use of OpenAI's GPT-4 Vision API to process slide content is also notable. However, the techniques themselves (RAG, LLMs) are not entirely novel and are being increasingly applied in educational contexts.

* **Significance:** The research has practical implications for online education and automated tutoring systems. It addresses the challenge of providing personalized feedback at scale, particularly in scenarios with high student-teacher ratios.  The findings offer insights into the strengths and weaknesses of AI-generated feedback and highlight the importance of multimodal feedback.  The study underscores the necessity of balancing personalized AI output with clarity, trustworthiness, and cognitive load management. It also emphasizes the importance of  grounding feedback within course materials.  However, the lack of statistically significant learning gain differences limits the paper's impact in definitively demonstrating superior learning outcomes. The small sample size in each experimental group further weakens its generalizability.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Addresses a relevant problem:** Providing scalable, personalized feedback is a persistent challenge in education.
    * **Clear research design:** The 2x2 factorial design allowed for a systematic comparison of different feedback modalities.
    * **Practical implementation:**  The SlideItRight system demonstrates a working prototype of the proposed approach.
    * **Mixed-methods approach:** Combining quantitative learning gains with qualitative student perceptions provides a more comprehensive understanding of the intervention's effects.
    * **Discussion focuses on key areas of improvement:**  The discussion section identifies areas needing further research, such as enhancing explanation clarity, building trust, and managing cognitive load.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Lack of statistically significant learning gains:**  The primary outcome measure (learning gains) did not show significant differences between conditions, which weakens the claim that the proposed approach is superior.
    * **Small sample size:**  The number of participants in each condition is relatively small, which reduces the statistical power of the study and limits the generalizability of the findings.
    * **Limited scope of assessment:**  The reliance on multiple-choice assessments may not fully capture nuanced learning or the ability to apply knowledge.
    * **Context Dependency:** Study context relies on a singular subject within engineering and e-learning. Results may not generalize to other fields.
    * **Human Feedback Variability:** Human generated feedback is inevitably subject to potential variance.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper contributes to a growing body of literature on the use of AI in education. The findings inform the design and implementation of future AI-powered feedback systems, particularly those incorporating multimodal information.  The paper also highlights the importance of considering student perceptions when developing educational technologies. Future developers can heed lessons in cognitive load management when providing AI feedback with relevant slide information.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6**.

*Rationale:* While the paper addresses a relevant problem and presents a practical implementation, the lack of statistically significant learning gains, coupled with the limited sample size, restricts its overall impact. The mixed-methods approach and the focus on student perceptions are valuable strengths, contributing to a more nuanced understanding of the challenges and opportunities of AI-generated feedback. The exploration of slide integration is a nice additional touch, however, the benefits aren't realized due to cognitive burden on the students. The study provides useful insights for future research and development but does not represent a breakthrough contribution in the field.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### ZeroSearch: Incentivize the Search Capability of LLMs without Searching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04588v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Sun, Zile Qiao, Jiayan Guo, Xuanbo Fan, Yingyan Hou, Yong Jiang, Pengjun Xie, Fei Huang, Yan Zhang
- **Abstract**: Effective information searching is essential for enhancing the reasoning and generation capabilities of large language models (LLMs). Recent research has explored using reinforcement learning (RL) to improve LLMs' search capabilities by interacting with live search engines in real-world environments. While these approaches show promising results, they face two major challenges: (1) Uncontrolled Document Quality: The quality of documents returned by search engines is often unpredictable, introducing noise and instability into the training process. (2) Prohibitively High API Costs: RL training requires frequent rollouts, potentially involving hundreds of thousands of search requests, which incur substantial API expenses and severely constrain scalability. To address these challenges, we introduce ZeroSearch, a reinforcement learning framework that incentivizes the search capabilities of LLMs without interacting with real search engines. Our approach begins with lightweight supervised fine-tuning to transform the LLM into a retrieval module capable of generating both relevant and noisy documents in response to a query. During RL training, we employ a curriculum-based rollout strategy that incrementally degrades the quality of generated documents, progressively eliciting the model's reasoning ability by exposing it to increasingly challenging retrieval scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ZeroSearch effectively incentivizes the search capabilities of LLMs using a 3B LLM as the retrieval module. Remarkably, a 7B retrieval module achieves comparable performance to the real search engine, while a 14B retrieval module even surpasses it. Furthermore, it generalizes well across both base and instruction-tuned models of various parameter sizes and is compatible with a wide range of RL algorithms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ZEROSEARCH, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to enhance the search capabilities of large language models (LLMs) without direct interaction with real-world search engines.  The core idea involves training an LLM to simulate a search engine, generating both relevant and noisy documents in response to queries. A curriculum-based rollout strategy progressively degrades the quality of these simulated documents during RL training, forcing the LLM-based policy model to reason and retrieve information from increasingly challenging retrieval scenarios.  By eliminating the need for expensive API calls to real search engines, ZEROSEARCH aims to overcome scalability issues and control document quality, leading to improved search capabilities and overall performance. Experiments demonstrate that ZEROSEARCH effectively enhances search capabilities, achieving competitive and even superior performance compared to models utilizing real search engines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of training LLMs to simulate search engines for RL is a novel approach. While using LLMs for document generation is not entirely new, the controlled generation of "noisy" documents and curriculum-based rollout is a significant contribution.  The separation of document generation from the RL policy training loop is a key differentiator, providing much better control and enabling a curriculum learning approach. This is a clear improvement over methods that directly interact with real-world search engines where the document quality is uncontrolled.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing the challenges of scalability and document quality control when applying RL to improve LLMs' search capabilities. ZEROSEARCH offers a cost-effective and stable alternative to real search engine interactions, paving the way for more extensive RL training. The experimental results showing competitive performance with real search engine-based methods strengthens the significance of this approach. The framework's generality across various base models, instruction-tuned models, and RL algorithms further emphasizes its value to the research community. The possibility of using a large (and more capable) simulated search engine than would be feasible in a real-world environment represents a real advancement.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The core idea is innovative and well-motivated.
    *   The curriculum rollout mechanism is effective in progressively challenging the policy model.
    *   The separation of concerns (document generation vs. policy learning) leads to better control.
    *   Experimental results are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach across diverse datasets and model types.
    *   Addresses a very practical issue: the high cost of API calls for RL in RAG systems.
    *   The simulation LLM size can be controlled, potentially leading to better scaling capabilities than directly querying an API.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The approach depends on the quality of the LLM used to simulate the search engine. While the paper demonstrates that even smaller LLMs can be effective, the effectiveness of ZEROSEARCH will likely be correlated with the knowledge and generative abilities of the simulation LLM. The dependence on the quality of the LLM used for the search engine simulator represents a potential weakness. While the quality of the simulator can be controlled, as argued in the paper, it's still an LLM, and prone to hallucination.
    *   There's a reliance on a lightweight SFT procedure to tailor the LLM search simulator which itself requires API calls during the data collection stage. This needs to be stated very clearly.
    *   Although not clearly discussed, depending on the implementation, it might not be easy to reproduce the experimental results.

*   **Potential Influence:** ZEROSEARCH has the potential to influence future research in RL-based LLM training, particularly for information retrieval tasks. It provides a valuable alternative to real-world search engine interaction and could lead to more scalable and controllable training pipelines. Other studies may leverage the framework or build upon its core concepts to further improve search and reasoning capabilities in LLMs.

*   **Justification of Score:** While the paper has some limitations, the core idea of using an LLM to simulate a search engine for reinforcement learning is genuinely novel and tackles a significant problem in the field. The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods, and the framework appears to be quite general. The curriculum rollout mechanism contributes significantly to the stability and effectiveness of the RL training process. The weaknesses primarily concern the dependence on the quality of the LLM search simulator and the initial data collection requirements.  Overall, the contribution is substantial and is therefore assigned a score of 7.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MonoCoP: Chain-of-Prediction for Monocular 3D Object Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04594v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihao Zhang, Abhinav Kumar, Girish Chandar Ganesan, Xiaoming Liu
- **Abstract**: Accurately predicting 3D attributes is crucial for monocular 3D object detection (Mono3D), with depth estimation posing the greatest challenge due to the inherent ambiguity in mapping 2D images to 3D space. While existing methods leverage multiple depth cues (e.g., estimating depth uncertainty, modeling depth error) to improve depth accuracy, they overlook that accurate depth prediction requires conditioning on other 3D attributes, as these attributes are intrinsically inter-correlated through the 3D to 2D projection, which ultimately limits overall accuracy and stability. Inspired by Chain-of-Thought (CoT) in large language models (LLMs), this paper proposes MonoCoP, which leverages a Chain-of-Prediction (CoP) to predict attributes sequentially and conditionally via three key designs. First, it employs a lightweight AttributeNet (AN) for each 3D attribute to learn attribute-specific features. Next, MonoCoP constructs an explicit chain to propagate these learned features from one attribute to the next. Finally, MonoCoP uses a residual connection to aggregate features for each attribute along the chain, ensuring that later attribute predictions are conditioned on all previously processed attributes without forgetting the features of earlier ones. Experimental results show that our MonoCoP achieves state-of-the-art (SoTA) performance on the KITTI leaderboard without requiring additional data and further surpasses existing methods on the Waymo and nuScenes frontal datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MonoCoP: Chain-of-Prediction for Monocular 3D Object Detection" addresses the problem of accurately estimating 3D attributes (size, angle, depth) from a single 2D image, a task known as monocular 3D object detection (Mono3D). The paper argues that existing methods often overlook the intrinsic inter-correlations among these 3D attributes due to the 3D-to-2D projection. To mitigate this, the authors propose MonoCoP, which leverages a Chain-of-Prediction (CoP) approach. This involves sequentially predicting the 3D attributes and conditioning each prediction on the preceding ones. The key components of MonoCoP include: AttributeNet (AN) for learning attribute-specific features, Feature Propagation (FP) to pass these features along the chain, and Feature Aggregation (FA) using residual connections to maintain information from earlier attributes. The paper demonstrates that MonoCoP achieves state-of-the-art performance on several datasets (KITTI, Waymo, nuScenes), showcasing improved accuracy and stability compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using a "chain of prediction" for 3D attribute estimation in monocular 3D detection is indeed novel. The inspiration from the Chain-of-Thought (CoT) concept in large language models and adapting it to the 3D vision domain is interesting and potentially impactful.  The explicit modeling of the dependencies between the 3D attributes is a strong point.  However, sequential attribute prediction isn't completely unheard of in other vision tasks; the novelty here lies in the specific application and design within the Mono3D context.

* **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical importance of Mono3D in autonomous driving and robotics, where cost-effective solutions are highly desired. Improved accuracy and stability in attribute estimation, particularly depth, can have a substantial impact on downstream tasks like scene understanding and planning. The empirical results, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on several datasets, further supports the significance of the proposed approach. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of MonoCoP, confirming the efficacy of the design choices. However, the performance gain, while significant, might not be groundbreaking compared to previous improvements in the field. The reliance on a DETR-based framework limits the overall architectural choices.

* **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed architecture with explicit modeling of attribute inter-dependencies.
    *   Strong empirical results across multiple datasets.
    *   Thorough ablation studies and analysis of design choices.
    *   Inspiration derived from an important and trending research area (CoT in LLMs).
    *   Mathematical support to further validate its purpose.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While novel, the "chain of prediction" concept builds upon existing ideas of sequential processing.  The degree of genuine surprise is moderate.
    *   The implementation relies on a DETR-based framework. While DETR is powerful, exploring the CoP in other Mono3D frameworks could further highlight the approach's generality.
    *   The paper focuses on performance improvements.  It would be stronger if it also delved into the *interpretability* aspects:  Does the CoP provide any insight into *why* specific attributes are related and how this relationship manifests in the learned features?
    *   Limitations section could benefit from more discussion about the method's failure modes and edge cases.  When does the CoP break down?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in monocular 3D object detection by encouraging more explicit modeling of attribute inter-dependencies and exploring sequential prediction strategies. It can also inspire adaptations of CoT principles from the LLM domain to other vision tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to monocular 3D object detection by explicitly addressing the inter-correlations among 3D attributes using a chain-of-prediction framework. The strong empirical results, especially state-of-the-art performance on established benchmarks, justify a score of 7.  The originality of the approach and it's potential to inspire adaptations from LLM to vision task is great, but performance improvements could have been more substantial and the interpretability aspects are missing.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### OmniGIRL: A Multilingual and Multimodal Benchmark for GitHub Issue Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04606v1)
- **Authors**: Lianghong Guo, Wei Tao, Runhan Jiang, Yanlin Wang, Jiachi Chen, Xilin Liu, Yuchi Ma, Mingzhi Mao, Hongyu Zhang, Zibin Zheng
- **Abstract**: The GitHub issue resolution task aims to resolve issues reported in repositories automatically. With advances in large language models (LLMs), this task has gained increasing attention, and several benchmarks are proposed to evaluate the issue resolution ability of LLMs. However, existing benchmarks have three main limitations. First, current benchmarks focus on a single programming language, limiting the evaluation of issues from repositories across different languages. Second, they usually cover a narrow range of domains, which may fail to represent the diversity of real-world issues. Third, existing benchmarks rely solely on textual information in issue descriptions, overlooking multimodal information such as images in issues. In this paper, we propose OmniGIRL, a GitHub Issue ResoLution benchmark that is multilingual, multimodal, and multi-domain. OmniGIRL includes 959 task instances, which are collected from repositories across four programming languages (i.e., Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, and Java) and eight different domains. Our evaluation shows that current LLMs show limited performances on OmniGIRL. Notably, the best-performing model, GPT-4o, resolves only 8.6% of the issues. Besides, we find that current LLMs struggle to resolve issues requiring understanding images. The best performance is achieved by Claude-3.5-Sonnet, which resolves only 10.5% of the issues with image information. Finally, we analyze the reasons behind current LLMs' failure on OmniGIRL, providing insights for future improvements.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, and then a critical evaluation with a score and justification.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OmniGIRL, a new benchmark for evaluating the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically resolve issues reported in GitHub repositories.  OmniGIRL aims to address limitations in existing benchmarks by being multilingual (supporting Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, and Java), multimodal (including text and images), and multi-domain (covering various software development areas). The paper details the creation of the OmniGIRL dataset, which consists of 959 task instances collected from 15 open-source repositories.  It evaluates the performance of several state-of-the-art LLMs on OmniGIRL, finding that their performance is limited, particularly on issues requiring image understanding or cross-file modifications. The authors analyze failure modes, identifying specific challenges for LLMs, such as difficulty in parsing structured output and limitations in resolving cross-file dependencies. Finally, they provide insights for future research and development in this area.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the *combination* of multilingual, multimodal, and multi-domain aspects in a single GitHub issue resolution benchmark. While individual benchmarks might have touched on one or two of these aspects, OmniGIRL's integrated approach is a more realistic and comprehensive assessment of LLM capabilities in software engineering. The inclusion of image data is particularly noteworthy as it reflects a common practice in issue reporting but has been largely ignored by previous benchmarks.
*  **Significance:** The significance stems from the benchmark's potential to drive advancements in LLM-based automated issue resolution. By exposing the limitations of existing models on a diverse and realistic dataset, the paper highlights crucial areas for improvement. The multi-lingual aspect pushes research towards models that are more generalizable and robust across different programming ecosystems, reflecting the real-world heterogeneity of software projects. Furthermore, the failure analysis provides actionable insights for researchers to focus on specific weaknesses in LLM reasoning and code modification abilities.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Dataset:**  The paper describes a well-defined methodology for constructing the OmniGIRL dataset, including clear selection criteria for repositories and pull requests.
    *   **Multimodal Focus:**  The emphasis on multimodal inputs (text and images) is a significant step forward, reflecting the reality of issue reporting practices.
    *   **Detailed Failure Analysis:**  The investigation into why LLMs fail on OmniGIRL provides valuable insights for future research directions. The parsing errors and cross-file modification challenges are well-articulated.
    *   **Open Availability:**  The open-source availability of the code and data is crucial for reproducibility and further research.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Visual Input Complexity:** While the inclusion of images is a plus, the paper acknowledges that the study of issues requiring visual understanding remains in its early stages. A deeper investigation into the types of visual information and their impact on LLM performance would be beneficial. The dataset, while a decent size for benchmark development, is still relatively small in size compared to a potential full-scale analysis dataset.

    *   **Evaluation Metric Scope:** The evaluation metrics are largely centered on resolution rate, apply rate, and cost. Further investigation on the specific nuances of correctness would significantly boost the analysis. For example, investigating issues such as "almost correct" outputs or outputs that follow the desired effect but perform the operation incorrectly in the context of the underlying algorithm would be of high importance.

    *   **Generalizability across modalities:** Though the multi-modal approach is an excellent addition, it is not yet possible to accurately ascertain how the different LLMs interact between the different modalities as the modality resolution performance is measured individually. In particular, it is not possible to confidently ascertain if all LLMs are truly taking multimodal information into account.

*   **Potential Influence:** OmniGIRL is likely to become a standard benchmark for evaluating LLM-based issue resolution systems, similar to how SWE-bench has been used. The multi-faceted approach will encourage research towards more robust and generalizable models, with image-based context especially adding another element of importance.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper makes a solid contribution by presenting a novel, more comprehensive benchmark (OmniGIRL) for evaluating LLMs in GitHub issue resolution. It addresses the limitations of previous benchmarks in terms of language support, domain diversity, and especially the inclusion of multimodal data. The detailed creation process and failure analysis provide valuable insights. However, the evaluation of visual understanding could be more in-depth, the dataset size is constrained, and a richer scope of the performance of the LLMs is required for a more comprehensive assessment. As such, a score of 7.5 is appropriate, reflecting a significant contribution but with room for improvement in subsequent iterations and studies. The key is that OmniGIRL lays a strong foundation for future research and benchmarking in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Score Distillation Sampling for Audio: Source Separation, Synthesis, and Beyond
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04621v1)
- **Authors**: Jessie Richter-Powell, Antonio Torralba, Jonathan Lorraine
- **Abstract**: We introduce Audio-SDS, a generalization of Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) to text-conditioned audio diffusion models. While SDS was initially designed for text-to-3D generation using image diffusion, its core idea of distilling a powerful generative prior into a separate parametric representation extends to the audio domain. Leveraging a single pretrained model, Audio-SDS enables a broad range of tasks without requiring specialized datasets. In particular, we demonstrate how Audio-SDS can guide physically informed impact sound simulations, calibrate FM-synthesis parameters, and perform prompt-specified source separation. Our findings illustrate the versatility of distillation-based methods across modalities and establish a robust foundation for future work using generative priors in audio tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Score Distillation Sampling for Audio: Source Separation, Synthesis, and Beyond":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Audio-SDS, an adaptation of Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) for text-conditioned audio diffusion models. Unlike traditional SDS applications in image and 3D generation, Audio-SDS leverages a single, pre-trained audio diffusion model to perform various audio tasks *without requiring specialized datasets*. The paper demonstrates Audio-SDS on three tasks: tuning FM synthesizers based on text prompts, guiding physically informed impact sound simulations, and performing prompt-driven source separation. The method involves encoding audio signals, adding noise, and using the diffusion model to predict and correct the noise, effectively "distilling" the diffusion model's knowledge into the audio parameters. The authors propose modifications to the original SDS framework to improve stability and perceptual quality in the audio domain, including decoder-based SDS, spectrogram emphasis, and multi-step denoising.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core concept of adapting SDS from image/3D to audio is not entirely novel in itself. However, the paper demonstrates practical modifications to the SDS pipeline to improve audio quality, specifically decoder SDS, spectrogram weighting, and multi-step denoising. The tasks demonstrated, such as tuning FM synthesizers and guiding physical simulations, are somewhat innovative in the context of SDS. While prompt-guided source separation exists, the Audio-SDS approach offers a unified framework using a single pre-trained model. The primary novelty comes from combining these elements and demonstrating the applicability of SDS to audio in a practical way.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the *versatility* demonstrated by the framework. Showing that a single, pre-trained model can be adapted to various audio tasks without task-specific training datasets is potentially impactful. This simplifies the development process for new audio applications and could lower the barrier to entry for researchers. The ability to control sound synthesis and editing with textual prompts is also significant for creative applications. Source separation's application showcases the potential for semantic control over audio decomposition, a task of great practical importance. The experiments, while not ground-breaking, provide tangible results illustrating Audio-SDS's capabilities. The approach could be useful for low-resource settings where large datasets for each task are not available.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Unified Framework:** Audio-SDS offers a unified approach for diverse audio tasks.
    *   **Data Efficiency:** Reduces the need for task-specific datasets by leveraging pre-trained diffusion models.
    *   **Practical Modifications:**  Decoder-SDS, spectrogram emphasis, and multi-step denoising demonstrably improve results in the audio domain.
    *   **Clear Demonstrations:** The FM synthesis, impact simulation, and source separation examples are well-defined and illustrated.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination is valuable, the core idea of SDS isn't new.
    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** Performance is limited by the capabilities of the base diffusion model, potentially struggling with out-of-distribution prompts or complex audio scenes. The paper states this weakness.
    *   **Limited Baselines:** The paper mentions difficulty finding direct comparisons due to its generalized approach, however, a more thorough comparison to task-specific SoTAs, even if retrained, would be valuable.
    *   **Optimization Sensitivities:** Like many SDS approaches, Audio-SDS can be sensitive to hyperparameter tuning.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper could inspire further research into adapting SDS and related distillation techniques for various audio applications. The framework’s versatility might lead to its adoption in areas such as sound design, audio editing, and interactive sound generation.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper introduces a practical and versatile framework for audio processing based on SDS. While the core concept isn’t radically new, the demonstrated improvements to the SDS pipeline for audio, the variety of tasks showcased, and the framework's potential to reduce reliance on task-specific training data justify a positive assessment. The main limitations stem from the inherent constraints of SDS and the reliance on the pre-trained model. Further refinement and broader adoption will determine the long-term impact of Audio-SDS.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 6/10

### PrimitiveAnything: Human-Crafted 3D Primitive Assembly Generation with Auto-Regressive Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04622v1)
- **Authors**: Jingwen Ye, Yuze He, Yanning Zhou, Yiqin Zhu, Kaiwen Xiao, Yong-Jin Liu, Wei Yang, Xiao Han
- **Abstract**: Shape primitive abstraction, which decomposes complex 3D shapes into simple geometric elements, plays a crucial role in human visual cognition and has broad applications in computer vision and graphics. While recent advances in 3D content generation have shown remarkable progress, existing primitive abstraction methods either rely on geometric optimization with limited semantic understanding or learn from small-scale, category-specific datasets, struggling to generalize across diverse shape categories. We present PrimitiveAnything, a novel framework that reformulates shape primitive abstraction as a primitive assembly generation task. PrimitiveAnything includes a shape-conditioned primitive transformer for auto-regressive generation and an ambiguity-free parameterization scheme to represent multiple types of primitives in a unified manner. The proposed framework directly learns the process of primitive assembly from large-scale human-crafted abstractions, enabling it to capture how humans decompose complex shapes into primitive elements. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that PrimitiveAnything can generate high-quality primitive assemblies that better align with human perception while maintaining geometric fidelity across diverse shape categories. It benefits various 3D applications and shows potential for enabling primitive-based user-generated content (UGC) in games. Project page: https://primitiveanything.github.io
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "PrimitiveAnything" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PrimitiveAnything, a novel framework for 3D shape abstraction. It reformulates shape abstraction as a sequence generation task, where the goal is to decompose a complex 3D shape into an assembly of simple geometric primitives (cuboids, cylinders, ellipsoids) in a human-like way.  The key components include: an ambiguity-free primitive parameterization scheme (handling symmetries to ensure stable learning), a primitive transformer architecture (a decoder-only transformer conditioned on the input shape), and an auto-regressive generation pipeline (predicting the next primitive based on previously generated primitives and the input shape).  The framework is trained on a large-scale, human-annotated dataset of 3D shapes and their primitive abstractions. The results show that PrimitiveAnything can generate high-quality primitive assemblies that better align with human perception and maintain geometric fidelity across diverse shape categories. The paper also demonstrates the potential for using PrimitiveAnything in primitive-based 3D content generation, interfacing with existing text-to-3D and image-to-3D generative models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the reformulation of primitive shape abstraction as a sequence generation problem tackled by a transformer. This is a significant departure from traditional optimization-based fitting or direct regression approaches. The introduction of an ambiguity-free parameterization scheme for primitives is also a valuable contribution, addressing a critical challenge in representing primitives effectively.  While auto-regressive transformers have been used in 3D mesh generation, applying them to primitive abstraction, particularly with human-crafted data and a multi-primitive representation, is innovative.

* **Significance:** The significance lies in the ability to generate human-interpretable and structured 3D representations.  This has applications in areas like robotic manipulation, scene understanding, computer-aided design, and interactive modeling.  The potential for enabling primitive-based user-generated content in games due to storage efficiency and easy manipulation is also noteworthy. The work addresses a limitation of many existing 3D generative models, which often lack semantic structure. The extensive empirical validation and the large-scale dataset are valuable assets.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Novel Approach:** The sequence generation formulation for primitive abstraction is a fresh perspective.
    * **Ambituity-Free Parameterization:** The approach tackles the symmetry problem well and leads to more stable and accurate training.
    * **Large-Scale Dataset:** The human-annotated dataset addresses the limitations of previous methods that rely on category-specific, small-scale data.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The quantitative and qualitative comparisons demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods in terms of both geometric fidelity and human alignment.
    * **Generalizability:** The method shows good generalization across various shape categories (including the ability to tackle out-of-distribution shapes).
    * **Applications:** The demonstration of integration with text-to-3D models highlights the practical relevance of the work.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Primitive Set Limitations:** The method is limited to a predefined set of primitives (cuboids, cylinders, ellipsoids). While sufficient for many shapes, representing more complex geometric features might require expanding the primitive vocabulary.
    * **Dependency on Human Annotation:** The dependency on human-annotated data, while a strength, also presents a scaling challenge.  Automating the annotation process would be beneficial.
    * **Abstraction Level Diversity: **There is no control about the diversity in primitive assemblies. An object might be approximated by many primitives, or very few ones.
    * **Limited Texture Handling:** The work primarily focuses on geometry and does not directly address texture generation.

* **Potential Influence:** The work is likely to influence future research in 3D shape abstraction, content generation, and scene understanding. The sequence generation approach and the ambiguity-free parameterization scheme could be adopted in other contexts. The dataset could also be used as a benchmark for future primitive abstraction methods. The demonstration that large models can capture high-level human intentions opens the doors for a broader range of semantic 3D tools.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper introduces a novel and effective approach to 3D shape abstraction, addressing limitations of existing methods with a fresh formulation and a well-designed framework. The strengths significantly outweigh the weaknesses. While there are areas for future improvement, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field. Therefore, a score of 8 is justified. The impact is solid due to the ability to create more semantic and useful 3D representations that are easily manipulated and stored.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 8/10

### EchoInk-R1: Exploring Audio-Visual Reasoning in Multimodal LLMs via Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04623v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenghao Xing, Xiaowei Hu, Chi-Wing Fu, Wenhai Wang, Jifeng Dai, Pheng-Ann Heng
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have advanced perception across text, vision, and audio, yet they often struggle with structured cross-modal reasoning, particularly when integrating audio and visual signals. We introduce EchoInk-R1, a reinforcement learning framework that enhances such reasoning in MLLMs. Built upon the Qwen2.5-Omni-7B foundation and optimized with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), EchoInk-R1 tackles multiple-choice question answering over synchronized audio-image pairs. To enable this, we curate AVQA-R1-6K, a dataset pairing such audio-image inputs with multiple-choice questions derived from OmniInstruct-v1. EchoInk-R1-7B achieves 85.77% accuracy on the validation set, outperforming the base model, which scores 80.53%, using only 562 reinforcement learning steps. Beyond accuracy, EchoInk-R1 demonstrates reflective reasoning by revisiting initial interpretations and refining responses when facing ambiguous multimodal inputs. These results suggest that lightweight reinforcement learning fine-tuning enhances cross-modal reasoning in MLLMs. EchoInk-R1 is the first framework to unify audio, visual, and textual modalities for general open-world reasoning via reinforcement learning. Code and data are publicly released to facilitate further research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "EchoInk-R1: Exploring Audio-Visual Reasoning in Multimodal LLMs via Reinforcement Learning" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EchoInk-R1, a reinforcement learning framework designed to enhance audio-visual reasoning capabilities in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Built on top of Qwen2.5-Omni-7B, and optimized using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), EchoInk-R1 tackles multiple-choice question answering tasks using synchronized audio-image pairs. The authors curate AVQA-R1-6K, a new dataset from OmniInstruct-v1 specifically for audio-visual reasoning. Experimental results demonstrate that EchoInk-R1 achieves improved accuracy compared to the base model, showcasing its ability to revisit initial interpretations and refine responses based on multimodal inputs. The paper highlights "aha moments" where the model corrects itself through cross-modal understanding. The authors release the code and data publicly.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty in unifying modalities:** The paper presents a novel reinforcement learning framework for general reasoning across audio, visual, and textual modalities, which is a significant step towards addressing the limitations of existing MLLMs in integrated audio-visual reasoning. This distinguishes it from prior work that focuses on individual modalities or simple combinations. The focus on a general task instead of an isolated one is appreciable.
    *   **Dataset Creation:** AVQA-R1-6K dataset is a valuable contribution. It provides a dedicated resource for training and evaluating audio-visual reasoning in MLLMs. The careful curation from OmniInstruct-v1 ensures that the dataset is relevant and challenging.
    *   **Methodological Rigor:** The use of GRPO, a proven reinforcement learning technique, is a sound choice. The paper provides sufficient details about the implementation and training process.
    *   **Demonstration of "Aha Moments":** The paper highlights the interesting phenomenon of self-corrective reasoning. The examples presented in the paper are convincing, and they point to a potentially important direction for improving MLLM reasoning capabilities.
    *   **Clear results and open source**: A tangible advantage is seen in validation set results, and public release of code and data is always a positive

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** While the framework is presented as "general," the experiments are limited to multiple-choice question answering. Demonstrating its effectiveness across a broader range of tasks (e.g., open-ended question answering, generation tasks) would strengthen the claim of generality.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The improvement over the base model, while statistically significant, might be considered incremental. The 5.24% increase in accuracy needs to be carefully weighed against the computational cost of reinforcement learning and the complexity of the framework. The "aha" moments, while interesting, may not translate to substantial performance gains in more complex scenarios.

    * **Reliance on Underlying Model** It's likely that improvements are contingent upon the improvements of base models such as Qwen.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it directly addresses a crucial limitation in MLLMs: the lack of robust multimodal reasoning, particularly when integrating audio and visual cues. The reinforcement learning approach and the demonstration of self-corrective reasoning provide valuable insights into how to improve MLLMs' reasoning abilities. The dataset provides a useful benchmark and is a step toward more generalized models.
    This work also opens the door for more research towards general framework for multimodal data.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7.
The paper exhibits good novelty in unifying audio-visual-text reasoning in a general task and provides a useful dataset and clear examples. The use of reinforcement learning for this purpose is also well-motivated. However, the gains in comparison to the complexity are potentially slim, and it would benefit from a broader task range to justify the "general" claim, making the overall impact relatively constrained.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 7/10
